     3→## 20260219 - Test infrastructure: fixture workspaces and test args

     5→### Context
     6→
     7→tspec has 255 unit tests but zero integration tests that actually build real packages.

    10→Three project structures need test coverage:
    11→1. **POP** — Plain Old Package (single Cargo.toml, no workspace)
    12→2. **POP+WS** — POP with embedded workspace (like tspec itself)
    13→3. **POWS** — Pure Old Workspace (workspace root, no package)
    14→
    15→### Plan
    16→
    17→**dev1:** Add test selection flags to `tspec test`:
    18→- `--test <TARGET>` (repeatable) — select specific test targets
    19→- `--names/-n <NAME>...` — filter tests by name (OR-matched substrings via test harness)
    20→- `--list/-l` — list test targets and functions (grouped with counts)
    21→- `-- <ARGS>` — pass trailing args to test binary (--ignored, --exact, etc.)

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     2→use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→
     5→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     6→use crate::types::Spec;
     7→
     8→/// Load a spec from a TOML file
     9→pub fn load_spec(path: &Path) -> Result<Spec> {
    10→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(path)
    11→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read spec file: {}", path.display()))?;
    12→    let spec = parse_spec(&content)?;
    13→    Ok(spec)
    14→}
    15→
    16→/// Parse a spec from TOML string
    17→pub fn parse_spec(toml_str: &str) -> Result<Spec> {
    18→    toml::from_str(toml_str).context("failed to parse spec TOML")
    19→}
    20→
    21→/// Serialize a spec to TOML string (canonical form for hashing)
    22→pub fn serialize_spec(spec: &Spec) -> Result<String> {
    23→    toml::to_string(spec).context("failed to serialize spec")
    24→}
    25→
    26→/// Compute hash of a resolved spec (first 8 hex chars)
    27→pub fn hash_spec(spec: &Spec) -> Result<String> {
    28→    let toml_str = serialize_spec(spec)?;
    29→    let mut hasher = Sha256::new();
    30→    hasher.update(toml_str.as_bytes());
    31→    let result = hasher.finalize();
    32→    Ok(hex::encode(&result[..4]))
    33→}
    34→
    35→/// Extract spec name from path by stripping the .ts.toml (or .toml) suffix
    36→pub fn spec_name_from_path(path: &Path) -> String {
    37→    let filename = path
    38→        .file_name()
    39→        .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string())
    40→        .unwrap_or_default();
    41→
    42→    filename
    43→        .strip_suffix(TSPEC_SUFFIX)
    44→        .or_else(|| filename.strip_suffix(".toml"))
    45→        .unwrap_or(&filename)
    46→        .to_string()
    47→}
    48→
    49→/// Expand template placeholders in a spec's target_dir field.
    50→/// Returns None if target_dir is absent or empty.
```

     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::path::Path;
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::{Execute, current_package_name, resolve_package_arg};
     7→use crate::all::{build_all, print_summary};
     8→use crate::binary::strip_binary;
     9→use crate::cargo_build::build_package;
    10→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};
    11→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    12→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    13→
    14→/// Build package(s) with a translation spec
    15→#[derive(Args)]
    16→pub struct BuildCmd {
    17→    /// Package to build (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    18→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    19→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    20→    /// Package to build (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    21→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    22→    pub package: Option<String>,
    23→    /// Build all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    24→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    25→    pub workspace: bool,
    26→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to build with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    27→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    28→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    29→    /// Release build
    30→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    31→    pub release: bool,
    32→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    33→    #[arg(long)]
    34→    pub profile: Option<String>,
    35→    /// Strip symbols from binary after build
    36→    #[arg(short, long)]
    37→    pub strip: bool,
    38→    /// Stop on first failure (for all-packages mode)
    39→    #[arg(short, long)]
    40→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    41→}
    42→
    43→impl BuildCmd {
    44→    /// Resolve the effective profile from --release / --profile flags.
    45→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    46→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    47→            Some(p)
    48→        } else if self.release {
    49→            Some("release")
    50→        } else {
    51→            None
    52→        }
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→impl Execute for BuildCmd {
    57→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    58→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    59→
    60→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    61→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    62→            None
    63→        } else {
    64→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    65→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    66→                None => current_package_name(),
    67→            }
    68→        };
    69→
    70→        match resolved {
    71→            None => {
    72→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    73→                let results = build_all(
    74→                    &workspace,
    75→                    &self.tspec,
    76→                    cli_profile,
    77→                    self.strip,
    78→                    self.fail_fast,
    79→                    flags,
    80→                );
    81→                Ok(print_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    82→            }
    83→            Some(name) => {
    84→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    85→                    let result = build_package(&name, None, cli_profile, flags)?;
    86→                    if self.strip {
    87→                        strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    88→                    }
    89→                } else {
    90→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
    91→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    92→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
    93→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
    94→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    95→                        let result = build_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, flags)?;
    96→                        if self.strip {
    97→                            strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    98→                        }
    99→                    }
   100→                }
   101→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   102→            }
   103→        }
   104→    }
   105→}

     5→use tspec::cmd::Execute;
     6→use tspec::find_paths::find_project_root;
     7→use tspec::types::{CargoFlags, Verbosity};
     8→
     9→fn main() -> Result<ExitCode, anyhow::Error> {
    10→    let cli = Cli::parse();
    11→    let flags = CargoFlags {
    12→        verbosity: Verbosity::from_count(cli.verbose),
    13→        jobs: cli.jobs,
    14→        extra_args: Vec::new(),
    15→    };
    16→
    17→    match cli.command {
    18→        Commands::Build(cmd) => {
    19→            return cmd.execute(&find_project_root()?, &flags);
    20→        }
    21→        Commands::Run(cmd) => {
    22→            return cmd.execute(&find_project_root()?, &flags);
    23→        }
    24→        Commands::Test(cmd) => {
    25→            return cmd.execute(&find_project_root()?, &flags);
    26→        }

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/fixture.rs",
  "content": "use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\nuse tempfile::TempDir;\n\n/// Directory containing fixture projects.\nfn fixtures_dir() -> PathBuf {\n    PathBuf::from(env!(\"CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR\")).join(\"tests/fixtures\")\n}\n\n/// Copy a fixture project into a fresh temporary directory.\n///\n/// Returns `(TempDir, PathBuf)` — the temp dir guard (drop removes it) and the\n/// path to the copied project root inside the temp dir.\npub fn copy_fixture(name: &str) -> (TempDir, PathBuf) {\n    let src = fixtures_dir().join(name);\n    assert!(src.is_dir(), \"fixture not found: {}\", src.display());\n\n    let tmp = TempDir::new().expect(\"failed to create temp dir\");\n    let dst = tmp.path().join(name);\n    copy_dir_recursive(&src, &dst).expect(\"failed to copy fixture\");\n    (tmp, dst)\n}\n\n/// Recursively copy a directory, skipping `target/` subdirectories.\nfn copy_dir_recursive(src: &Path, dst: &Path) -> std::io::Result<()> {\n    std::fs::create_dir_all(dst)?;\n    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(src)? {\n        let entry = entry?;\n        let file_name = entry.file_name();\n        if file_name == \"target\" {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let src_path = entry.path();\n        let dst_path = dst.join(&file_name);\n        if src_path.is_dir() {\n            copy_dir_recursive(&src_path, &dst_path)?;\n        } else {\n            std::fs::copy(&src_path, &dst_path)?;\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n"
}

// Use the installed tspec binary\n    \"tspec\".to_string()\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// POP fixture tests (run with: tspec test --test integration_test -- --ignored)\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n#[test]\n#[ignore]\nfn pop_spec_loads_correctly() {\n    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture(\"pop\");\n    let spec_path = project.join(\"tspec.ts.toml\");\n    let spec = load_spec(&spec_path).expect(\"failed to load spec\");\n\n    assert_eq!(spec.panic, Some(PanicMode::Abort));\n    assert_eq!(spec.cargo.profile, Some(\"release\".to_string()));\n}\n\n#[test]\n#[ignore]\nfn pop_cargo_build_succeeds() {\n    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture(\"pop\");\n\n    let output = Command::new(\"cargo\")\n        .args([\"build\"])\n        .current_dir(&project)\n        .output()\n        .expect(\"failed to run cargo build\");\n\n    assert!(\n        output.status.success(),\n        \"cargo build failed:\\n{}\",\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)\n    );\n}\n\n#[test]\n#[ignore]\nfn pop_tspec_build_succeeds() {\n    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture(\"pop\");\n\n    let output = Command::new(tspec_bin())\n        .args([\"build\", \".\"])\n        .current_dir(&project)\n        .output()\n        .expect(\"failed to run tspec build\");\n\n    assert!(\n        output.status.success(),\n        \"tspec build failed:\\n{}\",\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)\n    );\n}\n\n#[test]\n#[ignore]\nfn pop_tspec_compare_succeeds() {\n    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture(\"pop\");\n\n    // Build first so compare has something to compare\n    let build = Command::new(tspec_bin())\n        .args([\"build\", \".\"])\n        .current_dir(&project)\n        .output()\n        .expect(\"failed to run tspec build\");\n    assert!(\n        build.status.success(),\n        \"tspec build failed:\\n{}\",\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&build.stderr)\n    );\n\n    let output = Command::new(tspec_bin())\n        .args([\"compare\", \".\"])\n        .current_dir(&project)\n        .output()\n        .expect(\"failed to run tspec compare\");\n\n    assert!(\n        output.status.success(),\n        \"tspec compare failed:\\n{}\",\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)\n    );\n}\n"

> AGENT

There it is. `find_project_root()` walks up and when it hits the tspec root Cargo.toml (which has `[workspace]`), it returns that as the project root — even though `pop-fixture` is excluded from the workspace. It doesn't check the `exclude` list.

The fix should check whether the current directory is inside an excluded path. But actually, the more correct approach is: if we find a `[workspace]` Cargo.toml, check if the deepest `[package]` we already found is actually a member of that workspace. If it's excluded, the POP root should win.

Let me look at how workspace membership is determined.

> AGENT

Now let me check: does `cargo metadata` handle this correctly? If you run `cargo metadata` from inside an excluded directory, cargo should treat it as its own root. The real question is whether we should use `cargo metadata` or fix the manual walk-up logic.

The simplest correct fix: when `find_project_root()` finds a `[workspace]` Cargo.toml, check if the deepest `[package]` we found is excluded. If excluded, return the package root instead of the workspace root.

Let me look at how the workspace `exclude` field works in TOML.

    19→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml.display()))?;

    45→/// Find the project root by looking for Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package]
    46→/// For workspaces, returns the directory containing the workspace Cargo.toml
    47→/// For POPs (Plain Old Packages), returns the directory containing the package Cargo.toml
    48→pub fn find_project_root() -> Result<PathBuf> {
    49→    let mut dir = std::env::current_dir()?;
    50→    let mut package_root: Option<PathBuf> = None;
    51→
    52→    loop {
    53→        let cargo_toml = dir.join("Cargo.toml");
    54→        if cargo_toml.exists() {
    55→            let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)?;
    56→            // Workspace takes precedence
    57→            if has_toml_section_exact(&content, "workspace") {
    58→                return Ok(dir);
    59→            }
    60→            // Remember the first (deepest) package we find as potential POP root
    61→            if package_root.is_none() && has_toml_section_exact(&content, "package") {
    62→                package_root = Some(dir.clone());
    63→            }
    64→        }
    65→        if !dir.pop() {
    66→            // No workspace found, use the POP root if we found one
    67→            if let Some(root) = package_root {
    68→                return Ok(root);
    69→            }
    70→            bail!("could not find project root (no Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package])");

    87→/// Resolve package directory from optional name, defaulting to current directory
    88→/// If package is None, uses current directory (must contain Cargo.toml)
    89→/// If package is Some, looks up the package by name
    90→pub fn resolve_package_dir(workspace: &Path, package: Option<&str>) -> Result<PathBuf> {
    91→    match package {
    92→        Some(name) => find_package_dir(workspace, name),
    93→        None => {
    94→            let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
    95→            if cwd.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
    96→                Ok(cwd)
    97→            } else {
    98→                bail!(
    99→                    "not in a package directory (no Cargo.toml found). Use -p to specify a package."
   100→                )
   101→            }
   102→        }
   103→    }
   104→}
   105→
   106→/// Find a package's directory - tries as path first, then searches standard locations
   107→/// For POPs, checks if name matches the root package
   108→pub fn find_package_dir(project_root: &Path, name: &str) -> Result<PathBuf> {
   109→    // Try as path first (relative or absolute)
   110→    let as_path = PathBuf::from(name);
   111→    if as_path.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   112→        return Ok(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path));
   113→    }
   114→
   115→    // Check if name matches the root package
   116→    if let Ok(pkg_name) = get_package_name(project_root)
   117→        && pkg_name == name
   118→    {
   119→        return Ok(project_root.to_path_buf());
   120→    }
   121→
   122→    // For POPs, nothing else to search
   123→    if is_pop(project_root) {
   124→        bail!(
   125→            "package '{}' not found (this is a single-package project with package '{}')",
   126→            name,
   127→            get_package_name(project_root).unwrap_or_else(|_| "unknown".to_string())
   128→        );
   129→    }
   130→
   131→    // Workspace: search root-level members, then libs/, apps/, tools/
   132→    let root_path = project_root.join(name);
   133→    if root_path.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   134→        return Ok(root_path);
   135→    }
   136→
   137→    for prefix in ["libs", "apps", "tools"] {
   138→        let path = project_root.join(prefix).join(name);
   139→        if path.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   140→            return Ok(path);
   141→        }
   142→    }
   143→
   144→    // Special case: nested test crates like libs/rlibc-x2/tests
   145→    for prefix in ["libs", "apps"] {
   146→        for entry in std::fs::read_dir(project_root.join(prefix))
   147→            .into_iter()
   148→            .flatten()
   149→            .flatten()
   150→        {
   151→            let nested = entry.path().join("tests");
   152→            if nested.join("Cargo.toml").exists()
   153→                && let Ok(pkg_name) = get_package_name(&nested)
   154→                && pkg_name == name
   155→            {
   156→                return Ok(nested);
   157→            }
   158→        }
   159→    }
   160→
   161→    bail!("package '{}' not found", name);
   162→}
   163→
   164→/// Find the tspec for a package - tries as path first, then relative to pkg_dir
   165→/// Returns None if no tspec exists (plain cargo build will be used)
   166→pub fn find_tspec(pkg_dir: &Path, explicit: Option<&str>) -> Result<Option<PathBuf>> {
   167→    match explicit {
   168→        Some(name) => {
   169→            // Try as path first (relative to cwd or absolute)
   170→            let as_path = PathBuf::from(name);
   171→            if as_path.exists() {
   172→                return Ok(Some(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path)));
   173→            }
   174→
   175→            // Fallback: relative to package directory
   176→            let in_pkg = pkg_dir.join(name);
   177→            if in_pkg.exists() {
   178→                return Ok(Some(in_pkg));
   179→            }
   180→
   181→            // Try with TSPEC_SUFFIX if name has no extension
   182→            if !name.contains('.') {
   183→                let with_suffix = pkg_dir.join(format!("{}{}", name, TSPEC_SUFFIX));
   184→                if with_suffix.exists() {
   185→                    return Ok(Some(with_suffix));
   186→                }
   187→            }
   188→
   189→            bail!("tspec not found: {}", name);
   190→        }
   191→        None => {
   192→            let default = pkg_dir.join(format!("tspec{}", TSPEC_SUFFIX));
   193→            if default.exists() {
   194→                Ok(Some(default))
   195→            } else {
   196→                Ok(None) // No tspec = plain cargo build
   197→            }
   198→        }
   199→    }
   200→}
   201→
   202→/// Find multiple tspecs by glob patterns
   203→/// If no patterns given, defaults to "tspec*{TSPEC_SUFFIX}"
   204→/// Returns sorted list of paths, errors if none found
   205→pub fn find_tspecs(pkg_dir: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>> {
   206→    let default_pattern = format!("tspec*{}", TSPEC_SUFFIX);
   207→    let patterns: Vec<&str> = if patterns.is_empty() {
   208→        vec![&default_pattern]
   209→    } else {
   210→        patterns.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
   211→    };
   212→
   213→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   214→
   215→    for pattern in &patterns {
   216→        // Try as literal path first (relative to cwd or absolute)
   217→        let as_path = PathBuf::from(pattern);
   218→        if as_path.exists() && as_path.is_file() {
   219→            results.push(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path));
   220→            continue;
   221→        }
   222→
   223→        // Try as literal path relative to pkg_dir
   224→        let in_pkg = pkg_dir.join(pattern);
   225→        if in_pkg.exists() && in_pkg.is_file() {
   226→            results.push(in_pkg);
   227→            continue;
   228→        }
   229→
   230→        // Try as glob pattern in pkg_dir
   231→        let glob_pattern =
   232→            Pattern::new(pattern).with_context(|| format!("invalid glob pattern: {}", pattern))?;
   233→
   234→        let entries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(pkg_dir)
   235→            .with_context(|| format!("cannot read directory: {}", pkg_dir.display()))?
   236→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   237→            .filter(|e| {
   238→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   239→                e.path().is_file() && glob_pattern.matches(&name)
   240→            })
   241→            .map(|e| e.path())
   242→            .collect();
   243→
   244→        results.extend(entries);
   245→    }
   246→
   247→    // Remove duplicates and sort
   248→    results.sort();
   249→    results.dedup();
   250→
   251→    if results.is_empty() {
   252→        let pattern_list = patterns.join(", ");
   253→        bail!(
   254→            "no tspec files found matching '{}' in {}",
   255→            pattern_list,
   256→            pkg_dir.display()
   257→        );
   258→    }
   259→
   260→    Ok(results)
   261→}
   262→
   263→/// Get binary path for a build with a spec.
   264→/// `cli_profile` is the CLI-specified profile (None = debug default).
   265→pub fn get_binary_path(
   266→    workspace: &Path,
   267→    crate_name: &str,
   268→    spec: &Spec,
   269→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   270→    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
   271→) -> PathBuf {
   272→    let target = spec.cargo.target_triple.clone().or_else(|| {
   273→        spec.cargo
   274→            .target_json
   275→            .as_ref()
   276→            .and_then(|p| p.file_stem().map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string()))
   277→    });
   278→
   279→    // Determine profile directory: spec profile takes precedence, then CLI profile
   280→    let dir = match spec.cargo.profile.as_deref() {
   281→        Some(p) => profile_dir_name(p),
   282→        None => match cli_profile {
   283→            Some(p) => profile_dir_name(p),
   284→            None => "debug",
   285→        },
   286→    };
   287→
   288→    let base = match expanded_target_dir {
   289→        Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   290→        None => workspace.join("target"),
   291→    };
   292→
   293→    match target {
   294→        Some(t) => base.join(t).join(dir).join(crate_name),
   295→        None => base.join(dir).join(crate_name),
   296→    }
   297→}
   298→
   299→/// Get binary path for a simple build (no spec).
   300→/// `cli_profile` is the CLI-specified profile (None = debug default).
   301→pub fn get_binary_path_simple(
   302→    workspace: &Path,
   303→    crate_name: &str,
   304→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   305→) -> PathBuf {
   306→    let dir = match cli_profile {
   307→        Some(p) => profile_dir_name(p),
   308→        None => "debug",
   309→    };
   310→    workspace.join("target").join(dir).join(crate_name)
   311→}

```

> AGENT

Right. The problem is clear: `find_project_root()` at line 57-58 sees the tspec workspace's `[workspace]` and returns it immediately, without checking whether the current directory is excluded from that workspace.

The fix: when we find a `[workspace]` Cargo.toml and we already have a `package_root` (meaning cwd is inside an excluded or non-member package), we need to check the workspace's `exclude` list. If the package_root is excluded, return the package_root instead.

  "old_string": "/// Find the project root by looking for Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package]\n/// For workspaces, returns the directory containing the workspace Cargo.toml\n/// For POPs (Plain Old Packages), returns the directory containing the package Cargo.toml\npub fn find_project_root() -> Result<PathBuf> {\n    let mut dir = std::env::current_dir()?;\n    let mut package_root: Option<PathBuf> = None;\n\n    loop {\n        let cargo_toml = dir.join(\"Cargo.toml\");\n        if cargo_toml.exists() {\n            let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)?;\n            // Workspace takes precedence\n            if has_toml_section_exact(&content, \"workspace\") {\n                return Ok(dir);\n            }\n            // Remember the first (deepest) package we find as potential POP root\n            if package_root.is_none() && has_toml_section_exact(&content, \"package\") {\n                package_root = Some(dir.clone());\n            }\n        }\n        if !dir.pop() {\n            // No workspace found, use the POP root if we found one\n            if let Some(root) = package_root {\n                return Ok(root);\n            }\n            bail!(\"could not find project root (no Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package])\");\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Find the project root by looking for Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package]\n/// For workspaces, returns the directory containing the workspace Cargo.toml\n/// For POPs (Plain Old Packages), returns the directory containing the package Cargo.toml\n/// Respects workspace `exclude` — a package inside an excluded path is treated as a POP.\npub fn find_project_root() -> Result<PathBuf> {\n    let mut dir = std::env::current_dir()?;\n    let mut package_root: Option<PathBuf> = None;\n\n    loop {\n        let cargo_toml = dir.join(\"Cargo.toml\");\n        if cargo_toml.exists() {\n            let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)?;\n            // Workspace takes precedence — unless the package we found is excluded\n            if has_toml_section_exact(&content, \"workspace\") {\n                if let Some(ref pkg_root) = package_root {\n                    if is_excluded_from_workspace(&content, &dir, pkg_root) {\n                        return Ok(pkg_root.clone());\n                    }\n                }\n                return Ok(dir);\n            }\n            // Remember the first (deepest) package we find as potential POP root\n            if package_root.is_none() && has_toml_section_exact(&content, \"package\") {\n                package_root = Some(dir.clone());\n            }\n        }\n        if !dir.pop() {\n            // No workspace found, use the POP root if we found one\n            if let Some(root) = package_root {\n                return Ok(root);\n            }\n            bail!(\"could not find project root (no Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package])\");\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Check if a package directory is excluded from a workspace.\n/// `ws_content` is the workspace Cargo.toml content, `ws_dir` is the workspace root,\n/// and `pkg_dir` is the package directory to check.\nfn is_excluded_from_workspace(ws_content: &str, ws_dir: &Path, pkg_dir: &Path) -> bool {\n    let Ok(rel) = pkg_dir.strip_prefix(ws_dir) else {\n        return false;\n    };\n    let rel_str = rel.to_string_lossy();\n\n    // Parse workspace.exclude from the TOML\n    let Ok(doc) = ws_content.parse::<toml::Value>() else {\n        return false;\n    };\n    let Some(excludes) = doc\n        .get(\"workspace\")\n        .and_then(|w| w.get(\"exclude\"))\n        .and_then(|e| e.as_array())\n    else {\n        return false;\n    };\n\n    for exclude in excludes {\n        let Some(pattern) = exclude.as_str() else {\n            continue;\n        };\n        // Check if the package path starts with the exclude pattern\n        // e.g., exclude = [\"tests/fixtures\"] should match \"tests/fixtures/pop\"\n        if rel_str == pattern || rel_str.starts_with(&format!(\"{}/\", pattern)) {\n            return true;\n        }\n        // Also try glob matching\n        if let Ok(glob) = Pattern::new(pattern) {\n            if glob.matches(&rel_str) {\n                return true;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    false\n}"
}

> AGENT

Everything passes. Here's the summary of dev2:

**What was done:**

1. **POP fixture** — `tests/fixtures/pop/` with minimal `Cargo.toml`, `src/main.rs`, `tspec.ts.toml`
2. **Workspace config** — Added `exclude = ["tests/fixtures"]` to root `Cargo.toml`, `tests/fixtures/*/target/` to `.gitignore`
3. **Fixture helper** — `tests/fixture.rs` with `copy_fixture(name)` that copies fixtures to tmpdir (skipping `target/`)
4. **Integration tests** — `tests/integration_test.rs` with 4 `#[ignore]` tests: spec loading, cargo build, tspec build, tspec compare
5. **Bug fix** — `find_project_root()` now respects workspace `exclude` lists. Previously, running `tspec build` inside an excluded package (like `tests/fixtures/pop/`) would walk up to the parent workspace root and fail with "package not found". Now it correctly treats excluded packages as standalone POPs.

**Stats:** 272 unit tests + 4 integration tests, all passing. Clippy clean, fmt clean.

Ready for you to review. Should I commit?

> DEVELOPER

hmm, wait why was tspec looking beyond `tspec/tests/fixtures/pop/` we were in a valid crate why did it walk up the tree?

> AGENT

Because workspace members also have `[package]` in their Cargo.toml. Consider `tspec-build/` — it has:

```toml
[package]
name = "tspec-build"
```

If `find_project_root()` stopped at the first `[package]`, then running `tspec build .` from inside `tspec-build/` would treat it as a POP instead of finding the workspace root at `tspec/`. The workspace root is where `target/` lives and where cargo metadata resolves from — it's the correct project root for members.

So the walk-up logic is: remember the deepest `[package]` you find, but keep going because it might be a workspace member. If you hit a `[workspace]`, that's the real root. If you hit the filesystem root without finding a workspace, fall back to the remembered `[package]`.

The bug was just the missing `exclude` check — when the walk-up finds a `[workspace]`, it now verifies the remembered package isn't excluded before claiming it as the root.

> DEVELOPER

`cargo build` worked and it did the same thing but handled the exclude?

> AGENT

Yes, exactly. Cargo does the same walk-up but when it finds a `[workspace]`, it checks `members` and `exclude` to determine if the package actually belongs to that workspace. If excluded, cargo treats it as standalone.

Our `find_project_root()` was doing the walk-up but skipping that membership check — it just blindly returned any `[workspace]` it found. The fix brings it in line with how cargo handles it.

> DEVELOPER

sg, should we ignore or commit tests/fixtures/pop/Cargo.lock ?

> AGENT

Ignore it. It's a generated file specific to each build environment, and we don't publish the fixture as a crate. The fixture's `Cargo.toml` pins the edition — that's enough for reproducibility.

> AGENT

> DEVELOPER

Another thought, we probably need `--path xxx` parameter so tspec can be run on a fixture it or a copy without needing to cd into the directory does `cargo` have that I took a quick look and didn't see it?

> AGENT

Cargo has `--manifest-path <path/to/Cargo.toml>` — it points to a specific `Cargo.toml` rather than a directory. So `cargo build --manifest-path tests/fixtures/pop/Cargo.toml` works without cd'ing.

That's the closest equivalent. A tspec `--manifest-path` (or a friendlier `--path <dir>` that appends `/Cargo.toml` internally) would let you do:

```bash
tspec build --path tests/fixtures/pop
# instead of
cd tests/fixtures/pop && tspec build
```

It would also make integration tests cleaner — no need for `current_dir()` on the `Command`, just pass the path.

Worth noting in chores-6 as a future item? It's not blocking dev2-dev4 since `copy_fixture()` + `current_dir()` works, but it'd be nice for general usability too.

> DEVELOPER

yeah note it in chores-6, note that maybe we should use `--manifest-path` instead of `--path` I'm not sure why cargo doesn't use --path but it doesn't.

 /// Find the project root by looking for Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package]

 /// For POPs (Plain Old Packages), returns the directory containing the package Cargo.toml
+/// Respects workspace `exclude` — a package inside an excluded path is treated as a POP.
 pub fn find_project_root() -> Result<PathBuf> {

+/// Check if a package directory is excluded from a workspace.
+/// `ws_content` is the workspace Cargo.toml content, `ws_dir` is the workspace root,
+/// and `pkg_dir` is the package directory to check.
+fn is_excluded_from_workspace(ws_content: &str, ws_dir: &Path, pkg_dir: &Path) -> bool {
+    let Ok(rel) = pkg_dir.strip_prefix(ws_dir) else {
+        return false;
+    };
+    let rel_str = rel.to_string_lossy();
+
+    // Parse workspace.exclude from the TOML
+    let Ok(doc) = ws_content.parse::<toml::Value>() else {
+        return false;
+    };
+    let Some(excludes) = doc
+        .get("workspace")
+        .and_then(|w| w.get("exclude"))
+        .and_then(|e| e.as_array())
+    else {
+        return false;
+    };
+
+    for exclude in excludes {
+        let Some(pattern) = exclude.as_str() else {
+            continue;
+        };
+        // Check if the package path starts with the exclude pattern
+        // e.g., exclude = ["tests/fixtures"] should match "tests/fixtures/pop"
+        if rel_str == pattern || rel_str.starts_with(&format!("{}/", pattern)) {
+            return true;
+        }
+        // Also try glob matching
+        if let Ok(glob) = Pattern::new(pattern)
+            && glob.matches(&rel_str)
+        {
+            return true;
+        }
+    }
+
+    false
+}
+
 /// Check if a project root is a POP (Plain Old Package) vs a workspace
 pub fn is_pop(project_root: &Path) -> bool {
     let cargo_toml = project_root.join("Cargo.toml");

    58→Add a way to run tspec against a project without cd'ing into it. Cargo uses `--manifest-path <path/to/Cargo.toml>` (not `--path` — unclear why). We could follow cargo's convention (`--manifest-path`) or use `--path <dir>` for convenience. Would simplify integration tests and general usability. Not blocking dev2-dev4.
    59→
    60→### Documentation TODO
    61→
    62→Where and how to document the test fixtures (decide before release):
    63→- `README.md` — new `## Testing tspec` section documenting when/how to run integration tests
    64→- `tests/README.md` — purpose of the tests directory, overview of unit vs integration tests
    65→- `tests/fixtures/README.md` — purpose of fixtures, how copy_fixture() works
    66→- `tests/fixtures/pop/README.md` (and each fixture) — purpose of that specific fixture
    67→
    68→Open question: should the per-fixture READMEs be the "main" docs that README.md links to, or should README.md be self-contained with the READMEs as secondary?
    69→
    70→Full plan: `.claude/plans/enumerated-booping-curry.md`
    71→

     1→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     2→use tempfile::TempDir;
     3→
     4→/// Directory containing fixture projects.
     5→fn fixtures_dir() -> PathBuf {
     6→    PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("tests/fixtures")
     7→}
     8→
     9→/// Copy a fixture project into a fresh temporary directory.
    10→///
    11→/// Returns `(TempDir, PathBuf)` — the temp dir guard (drop removes it) and the
    12→/// path to the copied project root inside the temp dir.
    13→pub fn copy_fixture(name: &str) -> (TempDir, PathBuf) {
    14→    let src = fixtures_dir().join(name);
    15→    assert!(src.is_dir(), "fixture not found: {}", src.display());
    16→
    17→    let tmp = TempDir::new().expect("failed to create temp dir");
    18→    let dst = tmp.path().join(name);
    19→    copy_dir_recursive(&src, &dst).expect("failed to copy fixture");
    20→    (tmp, dst)
    21→}
    22→
    23→/// Recursively copy a directory, skipping `target/` subdirectories.
    24→fn copy_dir_recursive(src: &Path, dst: &Path) -> std::io::Result<()> {
    25→    std::fs::create_dir_all(dst)?;
    26→    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(src)? {
    27→        let entry = entry?;
    28→        let file_name = entry.file_name();
    29→        if file_name == "target" {
    30→            continue;
    31→        }
    32→        let src_path = entry.path();
    33→        let dst_path = dst.join(&file_name);
    34→        if src_path.is_dir() {
    35→            copy_dir_recursive(&src_path, &dst_path)?;
    36→        } else {
    37→            std::fs::copy(&src_path, &dst_path)?;
    38→        }
    39→    }
    40→    Ok(())
    41→}
    42→
```

    10→/// Package classification for behavior differences

    15→    /// libs/* - library crates

    18→    Tool,

> AGENT

`discover()` uses `MetadataCommand::new()` which uses cwd. Since integration tests use `Command::current_dir()` to run tspec, that won't work for calling `WorkspaceInfo::discover()` directly from the test process. But we can test workspace discovery via tspec commands — the important thing is that `tspec build -w` and `tspec build -p mylib` work.

> AGENT

The issue is `mylib` has no tspec file, so in workspace mode it falls into "Building mylib (no tspec)" instead of testing. It's trying to build and then failing because there's no binary for a library.

The fix is simple — mylib needs its own `tspec.ts.toml`, just like `tspec-build` has one in the real workspace.

     1→//! Batch operations on all workspace packages
     2→//!
     3→//! Provides build_all, run_all, test_all for operating on all workspace members.
     4→
     5→use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
     6→use std::process::ExitCode;
     7→
     8→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     9→
    10→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
    11→use crate::cargo_build::{build_package, test_package};
    12→use crate::compare::{SpecResult, compare_specs, print_comparison};
    13→use crate::find_paths::find_tspecs;
    14→use crate::run::run_binary;
    15→use crate::tspec::spec_name_from_path;
    16→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    17→use crate::workspace::{PackageKind, WorkspaceInfo};
    18→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
    19→
    20→/// Normalize tspec patterns for per-package matching in all-packages mode.
    21→///
    22→/// Strips directory components so shell-expanded paths from the workspace root
    23→/// don't leak into sub-packages. Filters out non-tspec shell expansions
    24→/// (e.g. `target/`, `tools/`) — only `.ts.toml` files and glob patterns are kept.
    25→///
    26→/// Returns `None` if all patterns were filtered out (likely shell expansion of a
    27→/// glob that matched non-tspec entries). The caller should warn about quoting.
    28→fn normalize_tspec_patterns(patterns: &[String]) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
    29→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    30→        return Some(Vec::new());
    31→    }
    32→    let filenames: Vec<String> = patterns
    33→        .iter()
    34→        .filter_map(|p| {
    35→            let name = Path::new(p)
    36→                .file_name()
    37→                .map(|f| f.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
    38→                .unwrap_or_else(|| p.clone());
    39→            // Keep glob patterns (unexpanded) and actual tspec files
    40→            let is_glob = name.contains('*') || name.contains('?') || name.contains('[');
    41→            let is_tspec = name.ends_with(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX);
    42→            if is_glob || is_tspec {
    43→                Some(name)
    44→            } else {
    45→                None
    46→            }
    47→        })
    48→        .collect();
    49→    if filenames.is_empty() && !patterns.is_empty() {
    50→        None // all patterns were filtered — likely shell expansion
    51→    } else {
    52→        Some(filenames)
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→/// Warn that shell glob expansion likely ate the tspec pattern.
    57→pub fn warn_shell_glob_expansion(patterns: &[String]) {
    58→    eprintln!(
    59→        "Warning: -t arguments ({}) don't look like tspec files.",
    60→        patterns.join(", ")
    61→    );
    62→    eprintln!("  The shell likely expanded your glob before tspec could see it.");
    63→    eprintln!("  Quote the pattern to prevent shell expansion: -t 'pattern*'");
    64→}
    65→
    66→/// Resolve tspec patterns for a workspace member.
    67→///
    68→/// Returns the matching spec paths, or an empty vec if no patterns match.
    69→/// When `patterns` is empty, returns an empty vec (caller should use default behavior).
    70→fn resolve_specs_for_member(member_path: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    71→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    72→        return Vec::new();
    73→    }
    74→    find_tspecs(member_path, patterns).unwrap_or_default()
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Extract a short spec label from an optional tspec path.
    78→fn spec_label(tspec: &Option<String>) -> String {
    79→    match tspec {
    80→        Some(path) => spec_name_from_path(Path::new(path)),
    81→        None => String::new(),
    82→    }
    83→}
    84→
    85→/// Result of a batch operation on a single package
    86→pub struct OpResult {
    87→    pub name: String,
    88→    pub spec: String,
    89→    pub success: bool,
    90→    pub message: String,
    91→    pub size: Option<u64>,
    92→}
    93→
    94→/// Build all workspace packages (excluding build tools)
    95→///
    96→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
    97→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
    98→pub fn build_all(
    99→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   100→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   101→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   102→    strip: bool,
   103→    fail_fast: bool,
   104→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   105→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   106→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   107→        Some(n) => n,
   108→        None => {
   109→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   110→            return Vec::new();
   111→        }
   112→    };
   113→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   114→
   115→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   116→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   117→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   118→            continue;
   119→        }
   120→
   121→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   122→
   123→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   124→            vec![None]
   125→        } else {
   126→            specs
   127→                .into_iter()
   128→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   129→                .collect()
   130→        };
   131→
   132→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   133→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   134→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   135→                Ok(build_result) => {
   136→                    if strip
   137→                        && member.has_binary
   138→                        && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)
   139→                    {
   140→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   141→                    }
   142→                    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path).ok();
   143→                    OpResult {
   144→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   145→                        spec,
   146→                        success: true,
   147→                        message: format!("{}", build_result.binary_path.display()),
   148→                        size,
   149→                    }
   150→                }
   151→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   152→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   153→                    spec,
   154→                    success: false,
   155→                    message: e.to_string(),
   156→                    size: None,
   157→                },
   158→            };
   159→
   160→            let failed = !result.success;
   161→            results.push(result);
   162→
   163→            if failed && fail_fast {
   164→                return results;
   165→            }
   166→        }
   167→    }
   168→
   169→    results
   170→}
   171→
   172→/// Run all app packages sequentially
   173→///
   174→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   175→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   176→pub fn run_all(
   177→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   178→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   179→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   180→    strip: bool,
   181→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   182→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   183→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   184→        Some(n) => n,
   185→        None => {
   186→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   187→            return Vec::new();
   188→        }
   189→    };
   190→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   191→
   192→    for member in workspace.runnable_members() {
   193→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   194→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   195→            continue;
   196→        }
   197→
   198→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   199→
   200→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   201→            vec![None]
   202→        } else {
   203→            specs
   204→                .into_iter()
   205→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   206→                .collect()
   207→        };
   208→
   209→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   210→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   211→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   212→                Ok(build_result) => {
   213→                    if strip && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path) {
   214→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   215→                    }
   216→                    match run_binary(&build_result.binary_path, &[]) {
   217→                        Ok(exit_code) => OpResult {
   218→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   219→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   220→                            success: true,
   221→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   222→                            size: None,
   223→                        },
   224→                        Err(e) => OpResult {
   225→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   226→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   227→                            success: false,
   228→                            message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   229→                            size: None,
   230→                        },
   231→                    }
   232→                }
   233→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   234→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   235→                    spec,
   236→                    success: false,
   237→                    message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   238→                    size: None,
   239→                },
   240→            };
   241→
   242→            results.push(result);
   243→        }
   244→    }
   245→
   246→    results
   247→}
   248→
   249→/// Test all workspace packages
   250→///
   251→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   252→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   253→pub fn test_all(
   254→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   255→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   256→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   257→    fail_fast: bool,
   258→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   259→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   260→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   261→        Some(n) => n,
   262→        None => {
   263→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   264→            return Vec::new();
   265→        }
   266→    };
   267→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   268→
   269→    // Test regular packages (excluding Test kind which needs special handling)
   270→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   271→        if member.kind == PackageKind::Test {
   272→            continue; // Handle test packages separately
   273→        }
   274→
   275→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   276→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   277→            continue;
   278→        }
   279→
   280→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   281→
   282→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   283→            vec![None]
   284→        } else {
   285→            specs
   286→                .into_iter()
   287→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   288→                .collect()
   289→        };
   290→
   291→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   292→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   293→            let result = match test_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   294→                Ok(()) => OpResult {
   295→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   296→                    spec,
   297→                    success: true,
   298→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   299→                    size: None,
   300→                },
   301→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   302→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   303→                    spec,
   304→                    success: false,
   305→                    message: e.to_string(),
   306→                    size: None,
   307→                },
   308→            };
   309→
   310→            let failed = !result.success;
   311→            results.push(result);
   312→
   313→            if failed && fail_fast {
   314→                return results;
   315→            }
   316→        }
   317→    }
   318→
   319→    // Handle test packages (like rlibc-x2-tests) - build and run all test binaries
   320→    for member in workspace.test_members() {
   321→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   322→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   323→            continue;
   324→        }
   325→
   326→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   327→
   328→        // Build the test package (builds all binaries)
   329→        let build_tspec = specs.first().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
   330→        let spec = spec_label(&build_tspec);
   331→        let build_result =
   332→            match build_package(&member.name, build_tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   333→                Ok(r) => r,
   334→                Err(e) => {
   335→                    results.push(OpResult {
   336→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   337→                        spec,
   338→                        success: false,
   339→                        message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   340→                        size: None,
   341→                    });
   342→                    if fail_fast {
   343→                        return results;
   344→                    }
   345→                    continue;
   346→                }
   347→            };
   348→
   349→        // Find and run all test binaries in the target directory
   350→        let profile_dir = crate::types::profile_dir_name(cli_profile.unwrap_or("debug"));
   351→        let target_dir = build_result.target_base.join(profile_dir);
   352→
   353→        // Look for binaries that end with "-tests" in the target directory
   354→        let test_binaries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(&target_dir)
   355→            .into_iter()
   356→            .flatten()
   357→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   358→            .filter(|e| {
   359→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   360→                let is_file = e.path().is_file();
   361→                let is_test = name.ends_with("-tests");
   362→                let is_executable = e
   363→                    .metadata()
   364→                    .map(|m| m.permissions().mode() & 0o111 != 0)
   365→                    .unwrap_or(false);
   366→                is_file && is_test && is_executable
   367→            })
   368→            .collect();
   369→
   370→        if test_binaries.is_empty() {
   371→            results.push(OpResult {
   372→                name: member.name.clone(),
   373→                spec: spec.clone(),
   374→                success: false,
   375→                message: "no test binaries found".to_string(),
   376→                size: None,
   377→            });
   378→            continue;
   379→        }
   380→
   381→        for entry in test_binaries {
   382→            let path = entry.path();
   383→            let bin_name = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   384→
   385→            print!("  Running {}... ", bin_name);
   386→
   387→            let result = match run_binary(&path, &[]) {
   388→                Ok(exit_code) => {
   389→                    if exit_code == 0 {
   390→                        println!("ok");
   391→                        OpResult {
   392→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   393→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   394→                            success: true,
   395→                            message: "ok".to_string(),
   396→                            size: None,
   397→                        }
   398→                    } else {
   399→                        println!("FAILED (exit {})", exit_code);
   400→                        OpResult {
   401→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   402→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   403→                            success: false,
   404→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   405→                            size: None,
   406→                        }
   407→                    }
   408→                }
   409→                Err(e) => {
   410→                    println!("FAILED");
   411→                    OpResult {
   412→                        name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   413→                        spec: spec.clone(),
   414→                        success: false,
   415→                        message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   416→                        size: None,
   417→                    }
   418→                }
   419→            };
   420→
   421→            let failed = !result.success;
   422→            results.push(result);
   423→
   424→            if failed && fail_fast {
   425→                return results;
   426→            }
   427→        }
   428→    }
   429→
   430→    results
   431→}
   432→
   433→/// A row for the summary table: package name, spec, and pre-formatted detail string.
   434→struct SummaryRow {
   435→    name: String,
   436→    spec: String,
   437→    detail: String,
   438→}
   439→
   440→/// Print a summary table with consistent formatting across all operations.
   441→///
   442→/// - `ws_name` — workspace/package name for the header
   443→/// - `cmd` — operation name ("TEST", "BUILD", "RUN", "COMPARE")
   444→/// - `detail_header` — column header for the detail column (e.g. "Status", "Status    Size", "Exit")
   445→/// - `rows` — pre-formatted rows
   446→/// - `footer` — footer line (e.g. "Test: 3 passed, 1 failed")
   447→fn print_summary_table(
   448→    ws_name: &str,
   449→    cmd: &str,
   450→    detail_header: &str,
   451→    rows: &[SummaryRow],
   452→    footer: &str,
   453→) {
   454→    let max_name_len = rows.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(7).max(7);
   455→    let has_spec = rows.iter().any(|r| !r.spec.is_empty());
   456→    let max_spec_len = if has_spec {
   457→        rows.iter().map(|r| r.spec.len()).max().unwrap_or(4).max(4)
   458→    } else {
   459→        0
   460→    };
   461→
   462→    println!();
   463→    print_header!(format!("{ws_name} {cmd} SUMMARY"));
   464→    if has_spec {
   465→        println!(
   466→            "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {detail_header}",
   467→            "Package",
   468→            "Spec",
   469→            nw = max_name_len,
   470→            sw = max_spec_len
   471→        );
   472→    } else {
   473→        println!(
   474→            "  {:width$}  {detail_header}",
   475→            "Package",
   476→            width = max_name_len
   477→        );
   478→    }
   479→
   480→    for row in rows {
   481→        if has_spec {
   482→            println!(
   483→                "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {}",
   484→                row.name,
   485→                row.spec,
   486→                row.detail,
   487→                nw = max_name_len,
   488→                sw = max_spec_len
   489→            );
   490→        } else {
   491→            println!(
   492→                "  {:width$}  {}",
   493→                row.name,
   494→                row.detail,
   495→                width = max_name_len
   496→            );
   497→        }
   498→    }
   499→
   500→    println!();
   501→    if !footer.is_empty() {
   502→        println!("  {footer}");
   503→    }
   504→    print_hline!();
   505→    println!();
   506→}
   507→
   508→/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)
   509→pub fn print_test_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   510→    let mut passed = 0;
   511→    let mut failed = 0;
   512→
   513→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   514→        .iter()
   515→        .map(|r| {
   516→            let detail = if r.success {
   517→                passed += 1;
   518→                "[PASS]".to_string()
   519→            } else {
   520→                failed += 1;
   521→                "[FAIL]".to_string()
   522→            };
   523→            SummaryRow {
   524→                name: r.name.clone(),
   525→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   526→                detail,
   527→            }
   528→        })
   529→        .collect();
   530→
   531→    print_summary_table(
   532→        name,
   533→        "TEST",
   534→        "Status",
   535→        &rows,
   536→        &format!("Test: {passed} passed, {failed} failed"),
   537→    );
   538→
   539→    if failed > 0 {
   540→        ExitCode::from(1)
   541→    } else {
   542→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   543→    }
   544→}
   545→
   546→/// Print a summary for build operations (OK/FAILED)
   547→pub fn print_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   548→    let mut ok_count = 0;
   549→    let mut failed_count = 0;
   550→
   551→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   552→        .iter()
   553→        .map(|r| {
   554→            let status = if r.success {
   555→                ok_count += 1;
   556→                "[ OK ]"
   557→            } else {
   558→                failed_count += 1;
   559→                "[FAIL]"
   560→            };
   561→            let size_str = r.size.map(format_size).unwrap_or_else(|| "--".to_string());
   562→            SummaryRow {
   563→                name: r.name.clone(),
   564→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   565→                detail: format!("{status}  {size_str:>6}"),
   566→            }
   567→        })
   568→        .collect();
   569→
   570→    print_summary_table(
   571→        name,
   572→        "BUILD",
   573→        "Status    Size",
   574→        &rows,
   575→        &format!("Build: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   576→    );
   577→
   578→    if failed_count > 0 {
   579→        ExitCode::from(1)
   580→    } else {
   581→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   582→    }
   583→}
   584→
   585→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   586→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   587→        format!("{:.1}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   588→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   589→        format!("{:.1}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   590→    } else {
   591→        format!("{}", bytes)
   592→    }
   593→}
   594→
   595→/// Result of a compare operation on a single package
   596→pub struct CompareResult {
   597→    pub op: OpResult,
   598→    pub specs: Vec<SpecResult>,
   599→}
   600→
   601→/// Compare all workspace packages that have binaries
   602→///
   603→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package discovers its own specs via default glob.
   604→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   605→pub fn compare_all(
   606→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   607→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   608→    fail_fast: bool,
   609→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   610→) -> Vec<CompareResult> {
   611→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   612→        Some(n) => n,
   613→        None => {
   614→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   615→            return Vec::new();
   616→        }
   617→    };
   618→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   619→
   620→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   621→        if !member.has_binary {
   622→            continue;
   623→        }
   624→
   625→        let spec_paths = if normalized.is_empty() {
   626→            find_tspecs(&member.path, &[]).unwrap_or_default()
   627→        } else {
   628→            let resolved = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   629→            if resolved.is_empty() {
   630→                continue;
   631→            }
   632→            resolved
   633→        };
   634→
   635→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   636→
   637→        let (op, specs) = match compare_specs(&member.name, &spec_paths, flags) {
   638→            Ok(spec_results) => (
   639→                OpResult {
   640→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   641→                    spec: String::new(),
   642→                    success: true,
   643→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   644→                    size: None,
   645→                },
   646→                spec_results,
   647→            ),
   648→            Err(e) => (
   649→                OpResult {
   650→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   651→                    spec: String::new(),
   652→                    success: false,
   653→                    message: e.to_string(),
   654→                    size: None,
   655→                },
   656→                Vec::new(),
   657→            ),
   658→        };
   659→
   660→        let failed = !op.success;
   661→        results.push(CompareResult { op, specs });
   662→
   663→        if failed && fail_fast {
   664→            break;
   665→        }
   666→    }
   667→
   668→    results
   669→}
   670→
   671→/// Print a summary for compare operations
   672→///
   673→/// With a single package, just prints its comparison table.
   674→/// With multiple packages, reprints all per-package tables then an overall OK/FAIL summary.
   675→pub fn print_compare_summary(name: &str, results: &[CompareResult]) -> ExitCode {
   676→    let has_failure = results.iter().any(|r| !r.op.success);
   677→
   678→    // Reprint per-package comparison tables together
   679→    for result in results {
   680→        if !result.specs.is_empty() {
   681→            print_comparison(&result.op.name, &result.specs);
   682→        }
   683→    }
   684→
   685→    // Only show overall COMPARE SUMMARY when there are multiple packages
   686→    if results.len() > 1 {
   687→        let mut ok_count = 0;
   688→        let mut failed_count = 0;
   689→
   690→        let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   691→            .iter()
   692→            .map(|r| {
   693→                let detail = if r.op.success {
   694→                    ok_count += 1;
   695→                    "[ OK ]".to_string()
   696→                } else {
   697→                    failed_count += 1;
   698→                    "[FAIL]".to_string()
   699→                };
   700→                SummaryRow {
   701→                    name: r.op.name.clone(),
   702→                    spec: r.op.spec.clone(),
   703→                    detail,
   704→                }
   705→            })
   706→            .collect();
   707→
   708→        print_summary_table(
   709→            name,
   710→            "COMPARE",
   711→            "Status",
   712→            &rows,
   713→            &format!("Compare: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   714→        );
   715→    }
   716→
   717→    if has_failure {
   718→        ExitCode::from(1)
   719→    } else {
   720→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   721→    }
   722→}
   723→
   724→/// Print a summary for run operations (shows exit codes, not pass/fail)
   725→pub fn print_run_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   726→    let mut error_count = 0;
   727→
   728→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   729→        .iter()
   730→        .map(|r| {
   731→            let detail = if r.success {
   732→                let code = r.message.strip_prefix("exit code: ").unwrap_or(&r.message);
   733→                format!("{code:>4}")
   734→            } else {
   735→                error_count += 1;
   736→                format!("ERROR: {}", r.message)
   737→            };
   738→            SummaryRow {
   739→                name: r.name.clone(),
   740→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   741→                detail,
   742→            }
   743→        })
   744→        .collect();
   745→
   746→    let footer = if error_count > 0 {
   747→        format!("Run: {error_count} error(s)")
   748→    } else {
   749→        String::new()
   750→    };
   751→
   752→    print_summary_table(name, "RUN", "Exit", &rows, &footer);
   753→
   754→    if error_count > 0 {
   755→        ExitCode::from(1)
   756→    } else {
   757→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   758→    }
   759→}
   760→
