```
    16→/// Built-in cargo profiles that don't require a `[profile.<name>]` definition.
    17→const BUILTIN_PROFILES: &[&str] = &["dev", "debug", "release", "test", "bench"];
    18→
    19→/// Validate that a custom profile is defined in the workspace Cargo.toml.
    20→/// Built-in profiles (dev, release, test, bench) are always valid.
    21→/// Custom profiles must have a `[profile.<name>]` section with `inherits`.
    22→pub fn validate_profile(profile: &str, workspace: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    23→    if BUILTIN_PROFILES.contains(&profile) {
    24→        return Ok(());
    25→    }
    26→    let cargo_toml_path = workspace.join("Cargo.toml");
    27→    let content = fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml_path)
    28→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml_path.display()))?;
    29→    let doc: toml::Value =
    30→        toml::from_str(&content).context("failed to parse workspace Cargo.toml")?;
    31→    if let Some(profiles) = doc.get("profile").and_then(|p| p.as_table())
    32→        && profiles.contains_key(profile)
    33→    {
    34→        return Ok(());
    35→    }
    36→    bail!(
    37→        "profile `{}` is not defined in {}. Custom profiles must be defined with \
    38→         `[profile.{}]` and an `inherits` field (e.g., `inherits = \"release\"`).",
    39→        profile,
    40→        cargo_toml_path.display(),
    41→        profile,
    42→    )
    43→}
    44→/// Check for spec settings that are likely misconfigurations.
    45→/// Returns a list of warning messages (printed at top and bottom of output).
    46→pub fn check_spec_misconfigurations(pkg_name: &str, spec: &Spec, pkg_dir: &Path) -> Vec<String> {
    47→    let mut warnings = Vec::new();
    48→    let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
    49→    let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join("src/main.rs").exists();
    50→
    51→    // -static with non-musl target (glibc + -static segfaults)
    52→    if has_linker_args {
    53→        let has_static = spec.linker.args.iter().any(|a| a == "-static");
    54→        let is_musl = spec
    55→            .cargo
    56→            .target_triple
    57→            .as_ref()
    58→            .is_some_and(|t| t.contains("musl"));
    59→        if has_static && !is_musl {
    60→            warnings.push(format!(
    61→                "Warning: -static linker arg without musl target for {}. \
    62→                 glibc + -static often segfaults; consider using a musl target triple.",
    63→                pkg_name
    64→            ));
    65→        }
    66→    }
    67→
    68→    // linker.args on lib-only package
    69→    if has_linker_args && !has_bin_target {
    70→        warnings.push(format!(
    71→            "Warning: linker.args ignored for {} (no binary target)",
    72→            pkg_name
    73→        ));
    74→    }
    75→
    76→    warnings
    77→}
    78→
    79→/// Print collected warnings again so they're visible after cargo output.
    80→pub fn reprint_warnings(warnings: &[String]) {
    81→    for w in warnings {
    82→        println!("{}", w);
    83→    }
    84→}
    85→
    86→pub fn warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale: bool) {
    87→    if had_stale {
    88→        println!("Warning: a stale build.rs was deleted, likely from a previous interrupted build");
    89→    }
    90→}
    91→
    92→/// Check if a build.rs file was generated by tspec.
    93→/// Matches either the marker comment or a file that only contains
    94→/// `cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=` println lines (marker-less variant).
    95→fn is_tspec_generated_build_rs(content: &str) -> bool {
    96→    if content.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER) {
    97→        return true;
    98→    }
    99→    // Fallback: file only contains fn main, cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin printlns, and braces
   100→    let dominated_by_link_args = content
   101→        .lines()
   102→        .map(|l| l.trim())
   103→        .filter(|l| !l.is_empty())
   104→        .all(|l| {
   105→            l == "fn main() {"
   106→                || l == "}"
   107→                || l.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER)
   108→                || l.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
   109→        });
   110→    dominated_by_link_args && content.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
   111→}
   112→
   113→/// Remove a stale tspec-generated build.rs left behind by an interrupted build.
   114→/// Returns true if a stale file was removed. Caller should print a warning after the build.
   115→pub fn remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(pkg_dir: &Path) -> bool {
   116→    let build_rs = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
   117→    if let Ok(content) = fs::read_to_string(&build_rs)
   118→        && is_tspec_generated_build_rs(&content)
   119→    {
   120→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs);
   121→        return true;
   122→    }
   123→    false
   124→}
   125→
   126→/// Result of a successful build
   127→pub struct BuildResult {
   128→    pub binary_path: PathBuf,
   129→    /// The effective target directory base (e.g., "target/foo" if target_dir was set)
   130→    pub target_base: PathBuf,
   131→}
   132→
   133→/// Unified cargo runner for build and test operations.
   134→///
   135→/// Handles spec loading, build.rs generation, command construction, and cleanup.
   136→/// `cli_profile` is the profile from the CLI (e.g., "release", "release-small").
   137→/// `None` means debug (default). `Some("release")` is equivalent to `--release`.
   138→pub fn run_cargo(
   139→    mode: CargoMode,
   140→    pkg_name: &str,
   141→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   142→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   143→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   144→) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   145→    let verbosity = flags.verbosity;
   146→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   147→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg_name)?;
   148→    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&pkg_dir, tspec)?;
   149→
   150→    // Resolve actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when pkg_name is a path)
   151→    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&pkg_dir)?;
   152→
   153→    // Clean up any stale tspec-generated build.rs from interrupted builds
   154→    let had_stale_build_rs = remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(&pkg_dir);
   155→
   156→    // Track if we generated a build.rs
   157→    let build_rs_path = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
   158→    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();
   159→
   160→    // Load spec once (if present) and compute target_dir
   161→    let (spec, expanded_td) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
   162→        let s = load_spec(path)?;
   163→        let name = spec_name_from_path(path);
   164→        let td = expand_target_dir(&s, &name)?;
   165→        (Some(s), td)
   166→    } else {
   167→        (None, None)
   168→    };
   169→
   170→    // Compute paths
   171→    let target_base = match &expanded_td {
   172→        Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   173→        None => workspace.join("target"),
   174→    };
   175→    let binary_path = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   176→        get_binary_path(
   177→            &workspace,
   178→            &pkg_name,
   179→            spec,
   180→            cli_profile,
   181→            expanded_td.as_deref(),
   182→        )
   183→    } else {
   184→        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, cli_profile)
   185→    };
   186→
   187→    // Validate the effective profile exists before invoking cargo
   188→    let effective_profile = spec
   189→        .as_ref()
   190→        .and_then(|s| s.cargo.profile.as_deref())
   191→        .or(cli_profile);
   192→    if let Some(profile) = effective_profile {
   193→        validate_profile(profile, &workspace)?;
   194→    }
   195→
   196→    // Check for misconfigurations before running cargo
   197→    let spec_warnings = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   198→        check_spec_misconfigurations(&pkg_name, spec, &pkg_dir)
   199→    } else {
   200→        Vec::new()
   201→    };
   202→
   203→    let verb = match mode {
   204→        CargoMode::Build => "Building",
   205→        CargoMode::Test => "Testing",
   206→    };
   207→
   208→    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo subcommand
   209→    let status = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   210→        let path = tspec_path.as_ref().unwrap();
   211→        println!("{} {} with spec {}", verb, pkg_name, path.display());
   212→
   213→        // -vv: print spec resolution details
   214→        if verbosity >= Verbosity::Debug {
   215→            let rel_path = path.strip_prefix(&workspace).unwrap_or(path);
   216→            println!("[debug] spec: {}", rel_path.display());
   217→            let tc = if let Some(tc) = &spec.toolchain {
   218→                format!("{} (from spec)", tc)
   219→            } else if requires_nightly(spec, mode) {
   220→                "nightly (auto-detected)".to_string()
   221→            } else {
   222→                "default".to_string()
   223→            };
   224→            println!("[debug] toolchain: {}", tc);
   225→            if let Some(td) = &expanded_td {
   226→                println!("[debug] target_dir: {} (expanded from spec)", td);
   227→            }
   228→            let eff = effective_profile.unwrap_or("debug");
   229→            let source = if spec.cargo.profile.is_some() {
   230→                "from spec"
   231→            } else if cli_profile.is_some() {
   232→                "from CLI"
   233→            } else {
   234→                "default"
   235→            };
   236→            println!("[debug] effective profile: {} ({})", eff, source);
   237→        }
   238→
   239→        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed
   240→        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
   241→        let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join("src/main.rs").exists();
   242→        if has_linker_args && has_bin_target && !had_build_rs {
   243→            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, spec)?;
   244→        }
   245→
   246→        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(spec, mode)?;
   247→        cmd.arg(mode.subcommand());
   248→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   249→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   250→
   251→        // Set spec path for tspec-build library to read in build.rs
   252→        cmd.env("TSPEC_SPEC_FILE", path.as_os_str());
   253→
   254→        apply_spec_to_command(
   255→            &mut cmd,
   256→            spec,
   257→            &workspace,
   258→            cli_profile,
   259→            expanded_td.as_deref(),
   260→        )?;
   261→
   262→        // For test mode, append -Zpanic_abort_tests to RUSTFLAGS if needed
   263→        if mode == CargoMode::Test && needs_panic_abort_tests(spec) {
   264→            let existing = cmd
   265→                .get_envs()
   266→                .find(|(k, _)| k == &"RUSTFLAGS")
   267→                .and_then(|(_, v)| v)
   268→                .map(|v| v.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   269→                .unwrap_or_default();
   270→            let new_flags = if existing.is_empty() {
   271→                "-Zpanic_abort_tests".to_string()
   272→            } else {
   273→                format!("{} -Zpanic_abort_tests", existing)
   274→            };
   275→            cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", new_flags);
   276→        }
   277→
   278→        // Pass global flags through to cargo (-v/-vv, -j N, extra args, etc.)
   279→        flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   280→
   281→        // -v: print command line and env vars
   282→        print_verbose_command(&cmd, verbosity);
   283→
   284→        cmd.status()
   285→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", mode.subcommand()))?
   286→    } else {
   287→        println!("{} {} (no tspec)", verb, pkg_name);
   288→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   289→        cmd.arg(mode.subcommand());
   290→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   291→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   292→        if let Some(p) = cli_profile {
   293→            match p {
   294→                "debug" | "dev" => {}
   295→                _ => {
   296→                    cmd.arg("--profile").arg(p);
   297→                }
   298→            }
   299→        }
   300→
   301→        // Pass global flags through to cargo (-v/-vv, -j N, extra args, etc.)
   302→        flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   303→
   304→        // -v: print command line even without spec
   305→        print_verbose_command(&cmd, verbosity);
   306→
   307→        cmd.status()
   308→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", mode.subcommand()))?
   309→    };
   310→
   311→    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)
   312→    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {
   313→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);
   314→    }
   315→
   316→    if !status.success() {
   317→        match &tspec_path {
   318→            Some(path) => {
   319→                let display_path = path.strip_prefix(&workspace).unwrap_or(path).display();
   320→                bail!(
   321→                    "cargo {} failed for `{}` with spec {}",
   322→                    mode.subcommand(),
   323→                    pkg_name,
   324→                    display_path
   325→                )
   326→            }
   327→            None => bail!("cargo {} failed for `{}`", mode.subcommand(), pkg_name),
   328→        }
   329→    }
   330→
   331→    if mode == CargoMode::Build {
   332→        println!("  {}", binary_path.display());
   333→    }
   334→    warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale_build_rs);
   335→    reprint_warnings(&spec_warnings);
   336→    Ok(BuildResult {
   337→        binary_path,
   338→        target_base,
   339→    })
   340→}
   341→
   342→/// Build a package with a spec, returns the binary path on success.
   343→pub fn build_package(
   344→    pkg_name: &str,
   345→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   346→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   347→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   348→) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   349→    run_cargo(CargoMode::Build, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)
   350→}
   351→
   352→/// Test a package with a spec.
   353→pub fn test_package(
   354→    pkg_name: &str,
   355→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   356→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   357→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   358→) -> Result<()> {
   359→    run_cargo(CargoMode::Test, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)?;
   360→    Ok(())
   361→}
   362→
   363→/// Plain `cargo build --release` with no spec lookup.
   364→/// Used by compare to produce a baseline build.
   365→pub fn plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name: &str, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   366→    build_package(pkg_name, None, Some("release"), flags)
   367→}
   368→
   369→/// Generate a temporary build.rs with scoped linker flags from tspec.toml
   370→pub fn generate_build_rs(path: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec) -> Result<()> {
   371→    let mut lines = vec![TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER.to_string(), "fn main() {".to_string()];
   372→
   373→    for arg in &spec.linker.args {
   374→        lines.push(format!(
   375→            "    println!(\"cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin={}={}\");",
   376→            crate_name, arg
   377→        ));
   378→    }
   379→
   380→    lines.push("}".to_string());
   381→    fs::write(path, lines.join("\n")).context("failed to write build.rs")?;
   382→    Ok(())
   383→}
   384→
   385→/// Cargo operation mode
   386→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
   387→pub enum CargoMode {
   388→    Build,
   389→    Test,
   390→}
   391→
   392→impl CargoMode {
   393→    fn subcommand(&self) -> &'static str {
   394→        match self {
   395→            CargoMode::Build => "build",
   396→            CargoMode::Test => "test",
   397→        }
   398→    }
   399→}
   400→
   401→/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain.
   402→/// For Test mode, panic=abort also needs nightly because `-Zpanic_abort_tests` is nightly-only.
   403→fn requires_nightly(spec: &Spec, mode: CargoMode) -> bool {
   404→    let panic_needs_nightly = match mode {
   405→        CargoMode::Test => spec
   406→            .panic
   407→            .map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some())
   408→            .unwrap_or(false),
   409→        CargoMode::Build => spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false),
   410→    };
   411→
   412→    let has_build_std = !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty();
   413→    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();
   414→
   415→    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable
   416→}
   417→
   418→/// Check if spec uses an abort-like panic mode that needs `-Zpanic_abort_tests` for testing.
   419→fn needs_panic_abort_tests(spec: &Spec) -> bool {
   420→    spec.panic
   421→        .map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some())
   422→        .unwrap_or(false)
   423→}
   424→
   425→/// Print command details at verbose level.
   426→fn print_verbose_command(cmd: &Command, verbosity: Verbosity) {
   427→    if verbosity >= Verbosity::Verbose {
   428→        let prog = cmd.get_program().to_string_lossy();
   429→        let args: Vec<String> = cmd
   430→            .get_args()
   431→            .map(|a| a.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   432→            .collect();
   433→        println!("[verbose] {} {}", prog, args.join(" "));
   434→        for (key, val) in cmd.get_envs() {
   435→            if let Some(v) = val {
   436→                println!(
   437→                    "[verbose] {}={}",
   438→                    key.to_string_lossy(),
   439→                    v.to_string_lossy()
   440→                );
   441→            }
   442→        }
   443→    }
   444→}
   445→
   446→/// Build the base cargo command (with toolchain if needed)
   447→fn build_cargo_command(spec: &Spec, mode: CargoMode) -> Result<Command> {
   448→    let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   449→
   450→    if let Some(tc) = &spec.toolchain {
   451→        cmd.arg(format!("+{}", tc));
   452→    } else if requires_nightly(spec, mode) {
   453→        cmd.arg("+nightly");
   454→    }
   455→
   456→    Ok(cmd)
   457→}
   458→
   459→/// Apply spec parameters to a cargo command.
   460→/// `cli_profile` is the CLI-specified profile (None = debug default).
   461→pub fn apply_spec_to_command(
   462→    cmd: &mut Command,
   463→    spec: &Spec,
   464→    workspace: &Path,
   465→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   466→    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
   467→) -> Result<()> {
   468→    // Set custom target directory if specified
   469→    if let Some(td) = expanded_target_dir {
   470→        cmd.arg("--target-dir")
   471→            .arg(workspace.join("target").join(td));
   472→    }
   473→
   474→    // Handle high-level panic mode (cargo -Z flag)
   475→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   476→        && let Some(z_flag) = panic_mode.cargo_z_flag()
   477→    {
   478→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(z_flag);
   479→    }
   480→
   481→    // Handle cargo config
   482→    let has_profile = spec.cargo.profile.is_some();
   483→    if let Some(ref profile) = spec.cargo.profile {
   484→        match profile.as_str() {
   485→            "debug" | "dev" => {
   486→                // Debug/dev is default, no flag needed
   487→            }
   488→            _ => {
   489→                cmd.arg("--profile").arg(profile);
   490→            }
   491→        }
   492→    }
   493→
   494→    if let Some(ref triple) = spec.cargo.target_triple {
   495→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(triple);
   496→    }
   497→
   498→    if let Some(ref path) = spec.cargo.target_json {
   499→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg("json-target-spec");
   500→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(path);
   501→    }
   502→
   503→    for flag in &spec.cargo.unstable {
   504→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(flag);
   505→    }
   506→
   507→    // Inject --config key=value pairs (flattened from potentially nested config)
   508→    for (key, value) in flatten_config(&spec.cargo.config) {
   509→        cmd.arg("--config").arg(format!("{}={}", key, value));
   510→    }
   511→
   512→    // If no profile in spec, fall back to CLI profile
   513→    if !has_profile && let Some(p) = cli_profile {
   514→        match p {
   515→            "debug" | "dev" => {} // default, no flag needed
   516→            _ => {
   517→                cmd.arg("--profile").arg(p);
   518→            }
   519→        }
   520→    }
   521→
   522→    // Collect rustc flags
   523→    let mut rustc_flags: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   524→
   525→    // Handle high-level panic mode (rustc -C flag)
   526→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   527→        && let Some(panic_value) = panic_mode.rustc_panic_value()
   528→    {
   529→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C panic={}", panic_value));
   530→    }
   531→
   532→    // Handle high-level strip mode
   533→    if let Some(strip_mode) = spec.strip
   534→        && let Some(strip_value) = strip_mode.rustc_strip_value()
   535→    {
   536→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C strip={}", strip_value));
   537→    }
   538→
   539→    if !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty() {
   540→        // -Z build-std is a cargo flag, not rustc
   541→        let crates_str = spec.cargo.build_std.join(",");
   542→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(format!("build-std={}", crates_str));
   543→    }
   544→
   545→    for flag in &spec.rustflags {
   546→        rustc_flags.push(flag.clone());
   547→    }
   548→
   549→    // Handle version script (generates file and adds linker arg)
   550→    if let Some(vs) = &spec.linker.version_script {
   551→        let vs_dir = match expanded_target_dir {
   552→            Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   553→            None => workspace.join("target"),
   554→        };
   555→        let _ = fs::create_dir_all(&vs_dir);
   556→        let version_script_path = vs_dir.join("tspec-version.script");
   557→
   558→        // Generate version script: { global: sym1; sym2; local: *; };
   559→        let globals = vs.global.join("; ");
   560→        let content = format!("{{ global: {}; local: {}; }};", globals, vs.local);
   561→
   562→        let mut f =
   563→            fs::File::create(&version_script_path).context("failed to create version script")?;
   564→        writeln!(f, "{}", content)?;
   565→
   566→        rustc_flags.push(format!(
   567→            "-C link-arg=-Wl,--version-script={}",
   568→            version_script_path.display()
   569→        ));
   570→    }
   571→
   572→    // Apply rustc flags (linker args from Args handled by generated build.rs)
   573→    if !rustc_flags.is_empty() {
   574→        cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", rustc_flags.join(" "));
   575→    }
   576→
   577→    Ok(())
   578→}
   579→
   580→#[cfg(test)]
   581→mod tests {
   582→    use super::*;
   583→    use tempfile::TempDir;
   584→
   585→    #[test]
   586→    fn remove_stale_tspec_build_rs_removes_marker_file() {
   587→        let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   588→        let build_rs = tmp.path().join("build.rs");
   589→        fs::write(
   590→            &build_rs,
   591→            "// Generated by tspec - do not edit\nfn main() {\n}\n",
   592→        )
   593→        .unwrap();
   594→
   595→        assert!(remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(tmp.path()));
   596→        assert!(!build_rs.exists());
   597→    }
   598→
   599→    #[test]
   600→    fn remove_stale_tspec_build_rs_preserves_user_file() {
   601→        let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   602→        let build_rs = tmp.path().join("build.rs");
   603→        fs::write(
   604→            &build_rs,
   605→            "fn main() {\n    println!(\"cargo:rerun-if-changed=src\");\n}\n",
   606→        )
   607→        .unwrap();
```

```
    13→/// Test package(s) with a translation spec
    14→#[derive(Args)]
    15→pub struct TestCmd {
    16→    /// Package to test (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    17→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    18→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    19→    /// Package to test (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    20→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    21→    pub package: Option<String>,
    22→    /// Test all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    23→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    24→    pub workspace: bool,
    25→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to test with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    26→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    27→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    28→    /// Release build
    29→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    30→    pub release: bool,
    31→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    32→    #[arg(long)]
    33→    pub profile: Option<String>,
    34→    /// Stop on first failure
    35→    #[arg(short, long)]
    36→    pub fail_fast: bool,

    39→    pub list: bool,
    40→    /// List available target names for use with --test
    41→    #[arg(long = "target-names")]
    42→    pub target_names: bool,
    43→    /// Filter by qualified test name, e.g. '-n _by_' matches 'ts_cmd::remove::tests::remove_by_index'
    44→    #[arg(short = 'n', long = "names", num_args = 1..)]
    45→    pub name_filter: Vec<String>,
    46→    /// Run only the named test target (use --target-names to see available names; repeatable)
    47→    #[arg(long = "test", value_name = "TARGET-NAME")]
    48→    pub test_target: Vec<String>,
    49→    /// Extra arguments passed after -- to the test binary (e.g., --ignored)
    50→    #[arg(last = true)]
    51→    pub test_args: Vec<String>,
    52→}
    53→
    54→impl TestCmd {
    55→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    56→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    57→            Some(p)
    58→        } else if self.release {
    59→            Some("release")
    60→        } else {
    61→            None
    62→        }
    63→    }
    64→}
    65→
    66→impl Execute for TestCmd {
    67→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    68→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    69→
    70→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    71→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    72→            None
    73→        } else {
    74→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    75→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    76→                None => current_package_name(),
    77→            }
    78→        };
    79→
    80→        if self.target_names {
    81→            return list_target_names(resolved.as_deref(), flags);
    82→        }
    83→
    84→        if self.list {
    85→            return list_tests(resolved.as_deref(), &self.name_filter, flags);
    86→        }
    87→
    88→        // Build extra_args from --names, --test, and trailing args
    89→        let has_extra = !self.name_filter.is_empty()
    90→            || !self.test_target.is_empty()
    91→            || !self.test_args.is_empty();
    92→        let flags = if has_extra {
    93→            let mut f = flags.clone();
    94→            for target in &self.test_target {
    95→                f.extra_args.push("--test".to_string());
    96→                f.extra_args.push(target.clone());
    97→            }
    98→            // Name filters and test_args both go after --
    99→            // The test harness accepts [FILTERS...] as OR-matched substrings
   100→            if !self.name_filter.is_empty() || !self.test_args.is_empty() {
   101→                f.extra_args.push("--".to_string());
   102→                f.extra_args.extend(self.name_filter.clone());
   103→                f.extra_args.extend(self.test_args.clone());
   104→            }
   105→            f
   106→        } else {
   107→            flags.clone()
   108→        };
   109→
   110→        match resolved {
   111→            None => {
   112→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
   113→
   114→                // When --test targets are specified in workspace mode, only test
   115→                // packages that actually have matching files in tests/
   116→                if !self.test_target.is_empty() {
   117→                    let members = workspace.buildable_members();
   118→                    let matching: Vec<_> = members
   119→                        .iter()
   120→                        .filter(|m| {
   121→                            self.test_target
   122→                                .iter()
   123→                                .any(|t| m.path.join("tests").join(format!("{t}.rs")).exists())
   124→                        })
   125→                        .collect();
   126→
   127→                    if matching.is_empty() {
   128→                        eprintln!(
   129→                            "No packages contain test targets: {}",
   130→                            self.test_target.join(", ")
   131→                        );
   132→                        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   133→                    }
   134→
   135→                    for member in &matching {
   136→                        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   137→                        test_package(&member.name, None, cli_profile, &flags)?;
   138→                    }
   139→                    return Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS);
   140→                }
   141→
   142→                let results =
   143→                    test_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.fail_fast, &flags);
   144→                Ok(print_test_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
   145→            }
   146→            Some(name) => {
   147→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
   148→                    test_package(&name, None, cli_profile, &flags)?;
   149→                } else {
   150→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
   151→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
   152→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
   153→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
   154→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
   155→                        test_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, &flags)?;
   156→                    }
   157→                }
   158→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   159→            }
   160→        }
   161→    }
   162→}
   163→
   164→/// Format a cargo test target header into a human-friendly label.
   165→///
   166→/// Cargo emits headers like:
   167→/// - `unittests src/lib.rs (target/debug/deps/tspec-abc123)` → unit tests (no --test name)
   168→/// - `tests/tspec_test.rs (target/debug/deps/tspec_test-abc123)` → integration test
   169→///
   170→/// For integration tests, we show the `--test` usable name prominently.
   171→fn format_target_header(raw: &str) -> String {
   172→    // Strip the "(target/...)" suffix for cleaner output
   173→    let label = if let Some(idx) = raw.find(" (") {
   174→        &raw[..idx]
   175→    } else {
   176→        raw
   177→    };
   178→
   179→    if label.starts_with("unittests ") {
   180→        // "unittests src/lib.rs" → unit tests, not selectable with --test
   181→        format!("unit tests ({})", label.strip_prefix("unittests ").unwrap())
   182→    } else if label.starts_with("tests/") {
   183→        // "tests/tspec_test.rs" → extract basename for --test
   184→        let basename = label
   185→            .strip_prefix("tests/")
   186→            .and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix(".rs"))
   187→            .unwrap_or(label);
   188→        format!("{} (--test {})", label, basename)
   189→    } else {
   190→        label.to_string()
   191→    }
   192→}
   193→
   194→/// List available target names for `--test`.
   195→///
   196→/// Runs `cargo test --no-run` and parses stderr for "Running tests/..." lines,
   197→/// extracting the basename (without .rs) as the target name.
   198→fn list_target_names(package: Option<&str>, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   199→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   200→
   201→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
   202→    cmd.arg("test").arg("--no-run");
   203→    if let Some(pkg) = package {
   204→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(pkg);
   205→    }
   206→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   207→    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   208→
   209→    let output = cmd.output().context("failed to run cargo test --no-run")?;
   210→
   211→    if !output.status.success() {
   212→        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   213→        eprintln!("{}", stderr);
   214→        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   215→    }
   216→
   217→    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   218→    let mut found = false;
   219→
   220→    println!("Available target names for --test:");
   221→    for line in stderr.lines() {
   222→        let trimmed = line.trim();
   223→        // --no-run emits "Executable tests/foo.rs (target/...)" for integration tests
   224→        if let Some(rest) = trimmed
   225→            .strip_prefix("Executable tests/")
   226→            .or_else(|| trimmed.strip_prefix("Running tests/"))
   227→        {
   228→            let file = if let Some(idx) = rest.find(" (") {
   229→                &rest[..idx]
   230→            } else {
   231→                rest
   232→            };
   233→            if let Some(name) = file.strip_suffix(".rs") {
   234→                println!("  {}    (tests/{})", name, file);
   235→                found = true;
   236→            }
   237→        }
   238→    }
   239→
   240→    if !found {
   241→        println!("  (none — no integration test files in tests/)");
   242→    }
   243→
   244→    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   245→}
   246→
   247→/// Run `cargo test -- --list` and format the output.
   248→///
   249→/// Groups test functions under their target headers, showing counts per target
   250→/// and a total. Skips targets with zero tests.
   251→fn list_tests(
   252→    package: Option<&str>,
   253→    name_filter: &[String],
   254→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   255→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   256→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   257→
   258→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
   259→    cmd.arg("test");
   260→    if let Some(pkg) = package {
   261→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(pkg);
   262→    }
   263→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   264→    cmd.arg("--");
   265→    cmd.args(name_filter);
   266→    cmd.arg("--list");
   267→    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   268→
   269→    let output = cmd.output().context("failed to run cargo test -- --list")?;
   270→
   271→    if !output.status.success() {
   272→        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   273→        eprintln!("{}", stderr);
   274→        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   275→    }
   276→
   277→    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   278→    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
   279→
   280→    // Parse target headers from stderr ("Running ..." and "Doc-tests ..." lines)
   281→    // Produce human-friendly labels that show the --test name when applicable.
   282→    let targets: Vec<String> = stderr
   283→        .lines()
   284→        .filter_map(|line| {
   285→            let trimmed = line.trim();
   286→            if trimmed.starts_with("Running ") {
   287→                let rest = trimmed.strip_prefix("Running ").unwrap();
   288→                Some(format_target_header(rest))
   289→            } else if trimmed.starts_with("Doc-tests ") {
   290→                Some(trimmed.to_string())
   291→            } else {
   292→                None
   293→            }
   294→        })
   295→        .collect();
   296→
   297→    // Parse stdout into groups split by "N tests, M benchmarks" summary lines
   298→    let mut groups: Vec<Vec<&str>> = Vec::new();
   299→    let mut current: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
   300→    for line in stdout.lines() {
   301→        if line.contains("tests,") && line.contains("benchmarks") {
   302→            groups.push(std::mem::take(&mut current));
   303→        } else if line.ends_with(": test") || line.ends_with(": bench") {
   304→            current.push(line);
   305→        }
   306→    }
   307→
   308→    // Print grouped output
   309→    let mut total = 0;
   310→    for (i, tests) in groups.iter().enumerate() {
   311→        if tests.is_empty() {
   312→            continue;
   313→        }
   314→        let unknown = "unknown target".to_string();
   315→        let header = targets.get(i).unwrap_or(&unknown);
   316→        println!("{} ({} tests)", header, tests.len());
   317→        for test in tests {
   318→            // Strip ": test" / ": bench" suffix for cleaner output
   319→            let name = test
   320→                .strip_suffix(": test")
   321→                .or_else(|| test.strip_suffix(": bench"))
   322→                .unwrap_or(test);
   323→            println!("  {}", name);
   324→        }
   325→        total += tests.len();
   326→        println!();
   327→    }
   328→
   329→    println!("{} tests total", total);
   330→    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   331→}
   332→
```

```
    29→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    30→
    31→/// Trait for command execution.
    32→pub trait Execute {
    33→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    34→}
    35→
    36→/// Resolve a `-p` argument (path or name) to the actual cargo package name.
    37→/// Returns Some(name) for a package, None if it resolves to a workspace root
    38→/// with no `[package]` section (meaning "operate on all packages").
    39→pub(crate) fn resolve_package_arg(pkg: &str) -> Result<Option<String>> {
    40→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    41→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg)?;
    42→    match get_package_name(&pkg_dir) {
    43→        Ok(name) => Ok(Some(name)),
    44→        Err(_) => Ok(None),
    45→    }
    46→}
    47→
    48→/// Helper for commands that execute a cargo subcommand.
    49→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(
    50→    subcommand: &str,
    51→    args: &[OsString],
    52→    project_root: &Path,
    53→    flags: &CargoFlags,
    54→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    55→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
    56→    cmd.arg(subcommand);
    57→    cmd.args(args);
    58→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
    59→    cmd.current_dir(project_root);
    60→    let status = cmd
    61→        .status()
    62→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", subcommand))?;
    63→    if status.success() {
    64→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    65→    } else {
    66→        bail!("cargo {} failed", subcommand);
    67→    }
    68→}
    69→
    70→/// Check if current directory is a package (has Cargo.toml with [package]).
    71→/// Returns None at a workspace root so that commands default to all-packages mode.
    72→pub(crate) fn current_package_name() -> Option<String> {
    73→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;
    74→    // At a workspace root, don't treat it as a single package
    75→    if cwd.join("Cargo.toml").exists() && !is_pop(&cwd) {
    76→        return None;
    77→    }
    78→    get_package_name(&cwd).ok()
    79→}
    80→
```

> AGENT

There it is. Line 75: `!is_pop(&cwd)` — for a POP+WS (which has both `[workspace]` and `[package]`), `is_pop` returns `false` (because it has `[workspace]`). So `current_package_name()` returns `None`, which means all-packages mode.

That's actually correct behavior for a POP+WS — `tspec test` at the workspace root should test all packages, same as tspec's own workspace. The issue is that `test_all` calls `test_package` for mylib but `run_cargo` for mylib sees "Building" in the output.

Actually, looking at the user's original output again more carefully — it says "Building mylib (no tspec)" with the **Build** verb. This means `CargoMode::Build` was used, not `CargoMode::Test`. But `test_all` calls `test_package` which uses `CargoMode::Test`...

Unless the output is from `tspec build` not `tspec test`? No, the user said `tspec test`. Let me look more carefully — oh wait, the second run I did also says "Building mylib". Something is wrong. Let me check if mylib's empty `[cargo]` spec is the problem.

```
     1→//! Workspace discovery and package classification
     2→//!
     3→//! Uses `cargo metadata` to discover workspace members and classify them
     4→//! by type (app, lib, tool, test, build tool).
     5→
     6→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     7→use cargo_metadata::MetadataCommand;
     8→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     9→
    10→/// Package classification for behavior differences
    11→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    12→pub enum PackageKind {
    13→    /// apps/* - application binaries, runnable
    14→    App,
    15→    /// libs/* - library crates
    16→    Lib,
    17→    /// tools/* - utility binaries
    18→    Tool,
    19→    /// */tests - test binary crates (special handling)
    20→    Test,
    21→    /// Build tools such as xtask or tspec, excluded by default
    22→    BuildTool,
    23→}
    24→
    25→/// Information about a workspace package
    26→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    27→pub struct PackageMember {
    28→    pub name: String,
    29→    pub path: PathBuf,
    30→    pub has_binary: bool,
    31→    pub kind: PackageKind,
    32→}
    33→
    34→/// Workspace information from cargo metadata
    35→pub struct WorkspaceInfo {
    36→    pub root: PathBuf,
    37→    pub members: Vec<PackageMember>,
    38→}
    39→
    40→impl WorkspaceInfo {
    41→    /// Workspace name derived from the root directory basename
    42→    pub fn name(&self) -> &str {
    43→        self.root
    44→            .file_name()
    45→            .and_then(|n| n.to_str())
    46→            .unwrap_or("workspace")
    47→    }
    48→
    49→    /// Discover workspace using cargo metadata
    50→    pub fn discover() -> Result<Self> {
    51→        let metadata = MetadataCommand::new()
    52→            .no_deps()
    53→            .exec()
    54→            .context("failed to run cargo metadata")?;
    55→
    56→        let root = metadata.workspace_root.as_std_path().to_path_buf();
    57→
    58→        let packages = metadata.workspace_packages();
    59→        let is_pop = packages.len() == 1;
    60→
    61→        let members: Vec<PackageMember> = packages
    62→            .iter()
    63→            .map(|pkg| {
    64→                let path = pkg
    65→                    .manifest_path
    66→                    .parent()
    67→                    .unwrap()
    68→                    .as_std_path()
    69→                    .to_path_buf();
    70→                let has_binary = pkg.targets.iter().any(|t| t.is_bin());
    71→                // POPs are always App — classify_package is for multi-package workspaces
    72→                let kind = if is_pop {
    73→                    PackageKind::App
    74→                } else {
    75→                    classify_package(&path, &pkg.name, &root)
    76→                };
    77→
    78→                PackageMember {
    79→                    name: pkg.name.clone(),
    80→                    path,
    81→                    has_binary,
    82→                    kind,
    83→                }
    84→            })
    85→            .collect();
    86→
    87→        Ok(WorkspaceInfo { root, members })
    88→    }
    89→
    90→    /// Get members excluding build tools such as xtask or tspec
    91→    pub fn buildable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
    92→        self.members
    93→            .iter()
    94→            .filter(|m| m.kind != PackageKind::BuildTool)
    95→            .collect()
    96→    }
    97→
    98→    /// Get members that can be run (apps only - have binaries and are runnable)
    99→    pub fn runnable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
   100→        self.members
   101→            .iter()
   102→            .filter(|m| m.has_binary && m.kind == PackageKind::App)
   103→            .collect()
   104→    }
   105→
   106→    /// Get test packages (special handling for rlibc-x2-tests etc.)
   107→    pub fn test_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
   108→        self.members
   109→            .iter()
   110→            .filter(|m| m.kind == PackageKind::Test)
   111→            .collect()
   112→    }
   113→}
   114→
   115→/// Classify a package based on its path, name, and workspace root
   116→fn classify_package(path: &Path, name: &str, workspace_root: &Path) -> PackageKind {
   117→    let path_str = path.to_string_lossy();

   122→    }

   139→    } else if path_str.contains("tools/") {
   140→        PackageKind::Tool
   141→    } else {
   142→        // Root-level members default to Lib
   143→        PackageKind::Lib
   144→    }
   145→}
```

> AGENT

Stopping. 

The root cause: `mylib` is classified as `PackageKind::Lib` (line 143 — root-level member defaults to Lib). In `test_all`, Lib packages go through `test_package` which calls `run_cargo(CargoMode::Test)`. But the verbose output clearly shows `cargo build` not `cargo test`. 

This is confusing — I need to investigate further. But you said stop, so waiting for your direction.

> AGENT

Found it. The bug is in `classify_package` at `workspace.rs:130`:

```rust
if path_str.contains("/tests") || name.ends_with("-tests") {
    return PackageKind::Test;
}
```

mylib's absolute path is `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tests/fixtures/pop-ws/mylib` — it contains `/tests` because the fixture lives under tspec's `tests/` directory. So mylib gets misclassified as `PackageKind::Test`, which sends it through the test-package-special-handling path that calls `build_package` instead of `test_package`.

This also explains why the integration tests pass — they copy to `/tmp/xxx/pop-ws/mylib` which has no `/tests` in the path.

The fix: check against the relative path from the workspace root, not the absolute path.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_018NThaezDMCGwBcCh9XGnvb
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/workspace.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Classify a package based on its path, name, and workspace root\nfn classify_package(path: &Path, name: &str, workspace_root: &Path) -> PackageKind {\n    let path_str = path.to_string_lossy();\n\n    // Root package of a workspace is the main app\n    if path == workspace_root {\n        return PackageKind::App;\n    }\n\n    // Build tools at workspace root level\n    if name == \"tspec\" || name == \"xt\" || name == \"xtask\" {\n        return PackageKind::BuildTool;\n    }\n\n    // Test packages\n    if path_str.contains(\"/tests\") || name.ends_with(\"-tests\") {\n        return PackageKind::Test;\n    }\n\n    // Categorize by directory\n    if path_str.contains(\"apps/\") {\n        PackageKind::App\n    } else if path_str.contains(\"libs/\") {\n        PackageKind::Lib\n    } else if path_str.contains(\"tools/\") {\n        PackageKind::Tool\n    } else {\n        // Root-level members default to Lib\n        PackageKind::Lib\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Classify a package based on its path relative to the workspace root.\nfn classify_package(path: &Path, name: &str, workspace_root: &Path) -> PackageKind {\n    // Root package of a workspace is the main app\n    if path == workspace_root {\n        return PackageKind::App;\n    }\n\n    // Build tools at workspace root level\n    if name == \"tspec\" || name == \"xt\" || name == \"xtask\" {\n        return PackageKind::BuildTool;\n    }\n\n    // Use path relative to workspace root for classification\n    // (absolute paths can contain misleading segments like tests/fixtures/)\n    let rel_str = path\n        .strip_prefix(workspace_root)\n        .map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned())\n        .unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_string_lossy().into_owned());\n\n    // Test packages\n    if rel_str.contains(\"/tests\") || rel_str.ends_with(\"tests\") || name.ends_with(\"-tests\") {\n        return PackageKind::Test;\n    }\n\n    // Categorize by directory\n    if rel_str.starts_with(\"apps/\") {\n        PackageKind::App\n    } else if rel_str.starts_with(\"libs/\") {\n        PackageKind::Lib\n    } else if rel_str.starts_with(\"tools/\") {\n        PackageKind::Tool\n    } else {\n        // Root-level members default to Lib\n        PackageKind::Lib\n    }\n}"
}
```

```
    26→use std::process::ExitCode;

    28→use crate::find_paths::{find_package_dir, find_project_root, get_package_name, is_pop};
    29→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    30→
    31→/// Trait for command execution.
    32→pub trait Execute {
    33→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    34→}
    35→
    36→/// Resolve a `-p` argument (path or name) to the actual cargo package name.
    37→/// Returns Some(name) for a package, None if it resolves to a workspace root
    38→/// with no `[package]` section (meaning "operate on all packages").
    39→pub(crate) fn resolve_package_arg(pkg: &str) -> Result<Option<String>> {
    40→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    41→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg)?;
    42→    match get_package_name(&pkg_dir) {
    43→        Ok(name) => Ok(Some(name)),
    44→        Err(_) => Ok(None),
    45→    }
    46→}
    47→
    48→/// Helper for commands that execute a cargo subcommand.
    49→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(
    50→    subcommand: &str,
    51→    args: &[OsString],
    52→    project_root: &Path,
    53→    flags: &CargoFlags,
    54→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    55→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
    56→    cmd.arg(subcommand);
    57→    cmd.args(args);
    58→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
    59→    cmd.current_dir(project_root);
    60→    let status = cmd
    61→        .status()
    62→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", subcommand))?;
    63→    if status.success() {
    64→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    65→    } else {
    66→        bail!("cargo {} failed", subcommand);
    67→    }
    68→}
    69→
    70→/// Check if current directory is a package (has Cargo.toml with [package]).
    71→/// Returns None at a workspace root so that commands default to all-packages mode.
    72→pub(crate) fn current_package_name() -> Option<String> {
    73→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;
    74→    // At a workspace root, don't treat it as a single package
    75→    if cwd.join("Cargo.toml").exists() && !is_pop(&cwd) {
    76→        return None;
    77→    }
    78→    get_package_name(&cwd).ok()
    79→}
    80→
```

```
     1→//! Batch operations on all workspace packages
     2→//!
     3→//! Provides build_all, run_all, test_all for operating on all workspace members.
     4→
     5→use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
     6→use std::process::ExitCode;
     7→
     8→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     9→
    10→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
    11→use crate::cargo_build::{build_package, test_package};
    12→use crate::compare::{SpecResult, compare_specs, print_comparison};
    13→use crate::find_paths::find_tspecs;
    14→use crate::run::run_binary;
    15→use crate::tspec::spec_name_from_path;
    16→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    17→use crate::workspace::{PackageKind, WorkspaceInfo};
    18→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
    19→
    20→/// Normalize tspec patterns for per-package matching in all-packages mode.
    21→///
    22→/// Strips directory components so shell-expanded paths from the workspace root
    23→/// don't leak into sub-packages. Filters out non-tspec shell expansions
    24→/// (e.g. `target/`, `tools/`) — only `.ts.toml` files and glob patterns are kept.
    25→///
    26→/// Returns `None` if all patterns were filtered out (likely shell expansion of a
    27→/// glob that matched non-tspec entries). The caller should warn about quoting.
    28→fn normalize_tspec_patterns(patterns: &[String]) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
    29→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    30→        return Some(Vec::new());
    31→    }
    32→    let filenames: Vec<String> = patterns
    33→        .iter()
    34→        .filter_map(|p| {
    35→            let name = Path::new(p)
    36→                .file_name()
    37→                .map(|f| f.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
    38→                .unwrap_or_else(|| p.clone());
    39→            // Keep glob patterns (unexpanded) and actual tspec files
    40→            let is_glob = name.contains('*') || name.contains('?') || name.contains('[');
    41→            let is_tspec = name.ends_with(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX);
    42→            if is_glob || is_tspec {
    43→                Some(name)
    44→            } else {
    45→                None
    46→            }
    47→        })
    48→        .collect();
    49→    if filenames.is_empty() && !patterns.is_empty() {
    50→        None // all patterns were filtered — likely shell expansion
    51→    } else {
    52→        Some(filenames)
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→/// Warn that shell glob expansion likely ate the tspec pattern.
    57→pub fn warn_shell_glob_expansion(patterns: &[String]) {
    58→    eprintln!(
    59→        "Warning: -t arguments ({}) don't look like tspec files.",
    60→        patterns.join(", ")
    61→    );
    62→    eprintln!("  The shell likely expanded your glob before tspec could see it.");
    63→    eprintln!("  Quote the pattern to prevent shell expansion: -t 'pattern*'");
    64→}
    65→
    66→/// Resolve tspec patterns for a workspace member.
    67→///
    68→/// Returns the matching spec paths, or an empty vec if no patterns match.
    69→/// When `patterns` is empty, returns an empty vec (caller should use default behavior).
    70→fn resolve_specs_for_member(member_path: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    71→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    72→        return Vec::new();
    73→    }
    74→    find_tspecs(member_path, patterns).unwrap_or_default()
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Extract a short spec label from an optional tspec path.
    78→fn spec_label(tspec: &Option<String>) -> String {
    79→    match tspec {
    80→        Some(path) => spec_name_from_path(Path::new(path)),
    81→        None => String::new(),
    82→    }
    83→}
    84→
    85→/// Result of a batch operation on a single package
    86→pub struct OpResult {
    87→    pub name: String,
    88→    pub spec: String,
    89→    pub success: bool,
    90→    pub message: String,
    91→    pub size: Option<u64>,
    92→}
    93→
    94→/// Build all workspace packages (excluding build tools)
    95→///
    96→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
    97→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
    98→pub fn build_all(
    99→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   100→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   101→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   102→    strip: bool,
   103→    fail_fast: bool,
   104→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   105→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   106→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   107→        Some(n) => n,
   108→        None => {
   109→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   110→            return Vec::new();
   111→        }
   112→    };
   113→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   114→
   115→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   116→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   117→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   118→            continue;
   119→        }
   120→
   121→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   122→
   123→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   124→            vec![None]
   125→        } else {
   126→            specs
   127→                .into_iter()
   128→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   129→                .collect()
   130→        };
   131→
   132→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   133→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   134→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   135→                Ok(build_result) => {
   136→                    if strip
   137→                        && member.has_binary
   138→                        && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)
   139→                    {
   140→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   141→                    }
   142→                    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path).ok();
   143→                    OpResult {
   144→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   145→                        spec,
   146→                        success: true,
   147→                        message: format!("{}", build_result.binary_path.display()),
   148→                        size,
   149→                    }
   150→                }
   151→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   152→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   153→                    spec,
   154→                    success: false,
   155→                    message: e.to_string(),
   156→                    size: None,
   157→                },
   158→            };
   159→
   160→            let failed = !result.success;
   161→            results.push(result);
   162→
   163→            if failed && fail_fast {
   164→                return results;
   165→            }
   166→        }
   167→    }
   168→
   169→    results
   170→}
   171→
   172→/// Run all app packages sequentially
   173→///
   174→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   175→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   176→pub fn run_all(
   177→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   178→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   179→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   180→    strip: bool,
   181→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   182→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   183→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   184→        Some(n) => n,
   185→        None => {
   186→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   187→            return Vec::new();
   188→        }
   189→    };
   190→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   191→
   192→    for member in workspace.runnable_members() {
   193→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   194→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   195→            continue;
   196→        }
   197→
   198→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);

   200→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {

   205→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   206→                .collect()
   207→        };
   208→
   209→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   210→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   211→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   212→                Ok(build_result) => {
   213→                    if strip && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path) {
   214→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   215→                    }
   216→                    match run_binary(&build_result.binary_path, &[]) {
   217→                        Ok(exit_code) => OpResult {

   237→                    message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   238→                    size: None,

   242→            results.push(result);

   336→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   337→                        spec,
   338→                        success: false,
   339→                        message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   340→                        size: None,
   341→                    });
   342→                    if fail_fast {
   343→                        return results;
   344→                    }
   345→                    continue;
   346→                }
   347→            };
   348→
   349→        // Find and run all test binaries in the target directory
   350→        let profile_dir = crate::types::profile_dir_name(cli_profile.unwrap_or("debug"));
   351→        let target_dir = build_result.target_base.join(profile_dir);
   352→
   353→        // Look for binaries that end with "-tests" in the target directory
   354→        let test_binaries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(&target_dir)
   355→            .into_iter()
   356→            .flatten()
   357→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   358→            .filter(|e| {
   359→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   360→                let is_file = e.path().is_file();
   361→                let is_test = name.ends_with("-tests");
   362→                let is_executable = e
   363→                    .metadata()
   364→                    .map(|m| m.permissions().mode() & 0o111 != 0)
   365→                    .unwrap_or(false);
   366→                is_file && is_test && is_executable
   367→            })
   368→            .collect();
   369→
   370→        if test_binaries.is_empty() {
   371→            results.push(OpResult {
   372→                name: member.name.clone(),
   373→                spec: spec.clone(),
   374→                success: false,
   375→                message: "no test binaries found".to_string(),
   376→                size: None,
   377→            });
   378→            continue;
   379→        }
   380→
   381→        for entry in test_binaries {
   382→            let path = entry.path();
   383→            let bin_name = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   384→
   385→            print!("  Running {}... ", bin_name);
   386→
   387→            let result = match run_binary(&path, &[]) {
   388→                Ok(exit_code) => {
   389→                    if exit_code == 0 {
   390→                        println!("ok");
   391→                        OpResult {
   392→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   393→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   394→                            success: true,
   395→                            message: "ok".to_string(),
   396→                            size: None,
   397→                        }
   398→                    } else {
   399→                        println!("FAILED (exit {})", exit_code);
   400→                        OpResult {
   401→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   402→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   403→                            success: false,
   404→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   405→                            size: None,
   406→                        }
   407→                    }
   408→                }
   409→                Err(e) => {
   410→                    println!("FAILED");
   411→                    OpResult {
   412→                        name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   413→                        spec: spec.clone(),
   414→                        success: false,
   415→                        message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   416→                        size: None,
   417→                    }
   418→                }
   419→            };
   420→
   421→            let failed = !result.success;
   422→            results.push(result);
   423→
   424→            if failed && fail_fast {
   425→                return results;
   426→            }
   427→        }
   428→    }
   429→
   430→    results
   431→}
   432→
   433→/// A row for the summary table: package name, spec, and pre-formatted detail string.
   434→struct SummaryRow {
   435→    name: String,
   436→    spec: String,
   437→    detail: String,
   438→}
   439→
   440→/// Print a summary table with consistent formatting across all operations.
   441→///
   442→/// - `ws_name` — workspace/package name for the header
   443→/// - `cmd` — operation name ("TEST", "BUILD", "RUN", "COMPARE")
   444→/// - `detail_header` — column header for the detail column (e.g. "Status", "Status    Size", "Exit")
   445→/// - `rows` — pre-formatted rows
   446→/// - `footer` — footer line (e.g. "Test: 3 passed, 1 failed")
   447→fn print_summary_table(
   448→    ws_name: &str,
   449→    cmd: &str,
   450→    detail_header: &str,
   451→    rows: &[SummaryRow],
   452→    footer: &str,
   453→) {
   454→    let max_name_len = rows.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(7).max(7);
   455→    let has_spec = rows.iter().any(|r| !r.spec.is_empty());
   456→    let max_spec_len = if has_spec {
   457→        rows.iter().map(|r| r.spec.len()).max().unwrap_or(4).max(4)
   458→    } else {
   459→        0
   460→    };
   461→
   462→    println!();
   463→    print_header!(format!("{ws_name} {cmd} SUMMARY"));
   464→    if has_spec {
   465→        println!(
   466→            "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {detail_header}",
   467→            "Package",
   468→            "Spec",
   469→            nw = max_name_len,
   470→            sw = max_spec_len
   471→        );
   472→    } else {
   473→        println!(
   474→            "  {:width$}  {detail_header}",
   475→            "Package",
   476→            width = max_name_len
   477→        );
   478→    }
   479→
   480→    for row in rows {
   481→        if has_spec {
   482→            println!(
   483→                "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {}",
   484→                row.name,
   485→                row.spec,
   486→                row.detail,
   487→                nw = max_name_len,
   488→                sw = max_spec_len
   489→            );
   490→        } else {
   491→            println!(
   492→                "  {:width$}  {}",
   493→                row.name,
   494→                row.detail,
   495→                width = max_name_len
   496→            );
   497→        }
   498→    }
   499→
   500→    println!();
   501→    if !footer.is_empty() {
   502→        println!("  {footer}");
   503→    }
   504→    print_hline!();
   505→    println!();
   506→}
   507→
   508→/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)
   509→pub fn print_test_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   510→    let mut passed = 0;
   511→    let mut failed = 0;
   512→
   513→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   514→        .iter()
   515→        .map(|r| {
   516→            let detail = if r.success {
   517→                passed += 1;
   518→                "[PASS]".to_string()
   519→            } else {
   520→                failed += 1;
   521→                "[FAIL]".to_string()
   522→            };
   523→            SummaryRow {
   524→                name: r.name.clone(),
   525→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   526→                detail,
   527→            }
   528→        })
   529→        .collect();
   530→
   531→    print_summary_table(
   532→        name,
   533→        "TEST",
   534→        "Status",
   535→        &rows,
   536→        &format!("Test: {passed} passed, {failed} failed"),
   537→    );
   538→
   539→    if failed > 0 {
   540→        ExitCode::from(1)
   541→    } else {
   542→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   543→    }
   544→}
   545→
   546→/// Print a summary for build operations (OK/FAILED)
   547→pub fn print_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   548→    let mut ok_count = 0;
   549→    let mut failed_count = 0;
   550→
   551→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   552→        .iter()
   553→        .map(|r| {
   554→            let status = if r.success {
   555→                ok_count += 1;
   556→                "[ OK ]"
   557→            } else {
   558→                failed_count += 1;
   559→                "[FAIL]"
   560→            };
   561→            let size_str = r.size.map(format_size).unwrap_or_else(|| "--".to_string());
   562→            SummaryRow {
   563→                name: r.name.clone(),
   564→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   565→                detail: format!("{status}  {size_str:>6}"),
   566→            }
   567→        })
   568→        .collect();
   569→
   570→    print_summary_table(
   571→        name,
   572→        "BUILD",
   573→        "Status    Size",
   574→        &rows,
   575→        &format!("Build: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   576→    );
   577→
   578→    if failed_count > 0 {
   579→        ExitCode::from(1)
   580→    } else {
   581→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   582→    }
   583→}
   584→
   585→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   586→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   587→        format!("{:.1}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   588→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   589→        format!("{:.1}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   590→    } else {
   591→        format!("{}", bytes)
   592→    }
   593→}
   594→
   595→/// Result of a compare operation on a single package
   596→pub struct CompareResult {
   597→    pub op: OpResult,
   598→    pub specs: Vec<SpecResult>,
   599→}
   600→
   601→/// Compare all workspace packages that have binaries
   602→///
   603→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package discovers its own specs via default glob.
   604→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   605→pub fn compare_all(
   606→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   607→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   608→    fail_fast: bool,
   609→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   610→) -> Vec<CompareResult> {
   611→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   612→        Some(n) => n,
   613→        None => {
   614→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   615→            return Vec::new();
   616→        }
   617→    };
   618→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   619→
   620→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   621→        if !member.has_binary {
   622→            continue;
   623→        }
   624→
   625→        let spec_paths = if normalized.is_empty() {
   626→            find_tspecs(&member.path, &[]).unwrap_or_default()
   627→        } else {
   628→            let resolved = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   629→            if resolved.is_empty() {
   630→                continue;
   631→            }
   632→            resolved
   633→        };
   634→
   635→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   636→
   637→        let (op, specs) = match compare_specs(&member.name, &spec_paths, flags) {
   638→            Ok(spec_results) => (
   639→                OpResult {
   640→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   641→                    spec: String::new(),
   642→                    success: true,
   643→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   644→                    size: None,
   645→                },
   646→                spec_results,
   647→            ),
   648→            Err(e) => (
   649→                OpResult {
   650→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   651→                    spec: String::new(),
   652→                    success: false,
   653→                    message: e.to_string(),
   654→                    size: None,
   655→                },
   656→                Vec::new(),
   657→            ),
   658→        };
   659→
   660→        let failed = !op.success;
   661→        results.push(CompareResult { op, specs });
   662→
   663→        if failed && fail_fast {
   664→            break;
   665→        }
   666→    }
   667→
   668→    results
   669→}
   670→
   671→/// Print a summary for compare operations
   672→///
   673→/// With a single package, just prints its comparison table.
   674→/// With multiple packages, reprints all per-package tables then an overall OK/FAIL summary.
   675→pub fn print_compare_summary(name: &str, results: &[CompareResult]) -> ExitCode {
   676→    let has_failure = results.iter().any(|r| !r.op.success);
   677→
   678→    // Reprint per-package comparison tables together
   679→    for result in results {
   680→        if !result.specs.is_empty() {
   681→            print_comparison(&result.op.name, &result.specs);
   682→        }
   683→    }
   684→
   685→    // Only show overall COMPARE SUMMARY when there are multiple packages
   686→    if results.len() > 1 {
   687→        let mut ok_count = 0;
   688→        let mut failed_count = 0;
   689→
   690→        let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   691→            .iter()
   692→            .map(|r| {
   693→                let detail = if r.op.success {
   694→                    ok_count += 1;
   695→                    "[ OK ]".to_string()
   696→                } else {
   697→                    failed_count += 1;
   698→                    "[FAIL]".to_string()
   699→                };
   700→                SummaryRow {
   701→                    name: r.op.name.clone(),
   702→                    spec: r.op.spec.clone(),
   703→                    detail,
   704→                }
   705→            })
   706→            .collect();
   707→
   708→        print_summary_table(
   709→            name,
   710→            "COMPARE",
   711→            "Status",
   712→            &rows,
   713→            &format!("Compare: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   714→        );
   715→    }
   716→
   717→    if has_failure {
   718→        ExitCode::from(1)
   719→    } else {
   720→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   721→    }
   722→}
   723→
   724→/// Print a summary for run operations (shows exit codes, not pass/fail)
   725→pub fn print_run_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   726→    let mut error_count = 0;
   727→
   728→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   729→        .iter()
   730→        .map(|r| {
   731→            let detail = if r.success {
   732→                let code = r.message.strip_prefix("exit code: ").unwrap_or(&r.message);
   733→                format!("{code:>4}")
   734→            } else {
   735→                error_count += 1;
   736→                format!("ERROR: {}", r.message)
   737→            };
   738→            SummaryRow {
   739→                name: r.name.clone(),
   740→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   741→                detail,
   742→            }
   743→        })
   744→        .collect();
   745→
   746→    let footer = if error_count > 0 {
   747→        format!("Run: {error_count} error(s)")
   748→    } else {
   749→        String::new()
   750→    };
   751→
   752→    print_summary_table(name, "RUN", "Exit", &rows, &footer);
   753→
   754→    if error_count > 0 {
   755→        ExitCode::from(1)
   756→    } else {
   757→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   758→    }
   759→}
```
