```
     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use glob::Pattern;
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→
     5→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     6→use crate::types::{Spec, profile_dir_name};
     7→
     8→/// Check if TOML content has an exact section header like `[workspace]` or `[package]`
     9→/// Only matches lines where the trimmed content equals `[section]` exactly
    10→fn has_toml_section_exact(content: &str, section: &str) -> bool {
    11→    let header = format!("[{}]", section);
    12→    content.lines().any(|line| line.trim() == header)
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Extract package name from Cargo.toml
    16→pub fn get_package_name(crate_dir: &Path) -> Result<String> {
    17→    let cargo_toml = crate_dir.join("Cargo.toml");
    18→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)
    19→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml.display()))?;
    20→
    21→    // Simple parsing - look for name = "..." in [package] section
    22→    let mut in_package = false;
    23→    for line in content.lines() {
    24→        let line = line.trim();
    25→        if line == "[package]" {
    26→            in_package = true;
    27→            continue;
    28→        }
    29→        if line.starts_with('[') {
    30→            in_package = false;
    31→            continue;
    32→        }
    33→        if in_package
    34→            && line.starts_with("name")
    35→            && let Some(eq_pos) = line.find('=')
    36→        {
    37→            let value = line[eq_pos + 1..].trim();
    38→            let value = value.trim_matches('"').trim_matches('\'');
    39→            return Ok(value.to_string());
    40→        }
    41→    }
    42→    bail!("could not find package name in {}", cargo_toml.display());
    43→}
    44→
    45→/// Find the project root by looking for Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package]
    46→/// For workspaces, returns the directory containing the workspace Cargo.toml
    47→/// For POPs (Plain Old Packages), returns the directory containing the package Cargo.toml
    48→/// Respects workspace `exclude` — a package inside an excluded path is treated as a POP.
    49→pub fn find_project_root() -> Result<PathBuf> {
    50→    let mut dir = std::env::current_dir()?;
    51→    let mut package_root: Option<PathBuf> = None;
    52→
    53→    loop {
    54→        let cargo_toml = dir.join("Cargo.toml");
    55→        if cargo_toml.exists() {
    56→            let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)?;
    57→            // Workspace takes precedence — unless the package we found is excluded
    58→            if has_toml_section_exact(&content, "workspace") {
    59→                if let Some(ref pkg_root) = package_root
    60→                    && is_excluded_from_workspace(&content, &dir, pkg_root)
    61→                {
    62→                    return Ok(pkg_root.clone());
    63→                }
    64→                return Ok(dir);
    65→            }
    66→            // Remember the first (deepest) package we find as potential POP root
    67→            if package_root.is_none() && has_toml_section_exact(&content, "package") {
    68→                package_root = Some(dir.clone());
    69→            }
    70→        }
    71→        if !dir.pop() {
    72→            // No workspace found, use the POP root if we found one
    73→            if let Some(root) = package_root {
    74→                return Ok(root);
    75→            }
    76→            bail!("could not find project root (no Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package])");
    77→        }
    78→    }
    79→}
    80→
    81→/// Check if a package directory is excluded from a workspace.
    82→/// `ws_content` is the workspace Cargo.toml content, `ws_dir` is the workspace root,
    83→/// and `pkg_dir` is the package directory to check.
    84→fn is_excluded_from_workspace(ws_content: &str, ws_dir: &Path, pkg_dir: &Path) -> bool {
    85→    let Ok(rel) = pkg_dir.strip_prefix(ws_dir) else {
    86→        return false;
    87→    };
    88→    let rel_str = rel.to_string_lossy();
    89→
    90→    // Parse workspace.exclude from the TOML
    91→    let Ok(doc) = ws_content.parse::<toml::Value>() else {
    92→        return false;
    93→    };
    94→    let Some(excludes) = doc
    95→        .get("workspace")
    96→        .and_then(|w| w.get("exclude"))
    97→        .and_then(|e| e.as_array())
    98→    else {
    99→        return false;
   100→    };
   101→
   102→    for exclude in excludes {
   103→        let Some(pattern) = exclude.as_str() else {
   104→            continue;
   105→        };
   106→        // Check if the package path starts with the exclude pattern
   107→        // e.g., exclude = ["tests/fixtures"] should match "tests/fixtures/pop"
   108→        if rel_str == pattern || rel_str.starts_with(&format!("{}/", pattern)) {
   109→            return true;
   110→        }
   111→        // Also try glob matching
   112→        if let Ok(glob) = Pattern::new(pattern)
   113→            && glob.matches(&rel_str)
   114→        {
   115→            return true;
   116→        }
   117→    }
   118→
   119→    false
   120→}
   121→
   122→/// Check if a project root is a POP (Plain Old Package) vs a workspace
   123→pub fn is_pop(project_root: &Path) -> bool {
   124→    let cargo_toml = project_root.join("Cargo.toml");
   125→    if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml) {
   126→        // POP has [package] but no [workspace]
   127→        has_toml_section_exact(&content, "package")
   128→            && !has_toml_section_exact(&content, "workspace")
   129→    } else {
   130→        false
   131→    }
   132→}
   133→
   134→/// Resolve package directory from optional name, defaulting to current directory
   135→/// If package is None, uses current directory (must contain Cargo.toml)
   136→/// If package is Some, looks up the package by name
   137→pub fn resolve_package_dir(workspace: &Path, package: Option<&str>) -> Result<PathBuf> {
   138→    match package {
   139→        Some(name) => find_package_dir(workspace, name),
   140→        None => {
   141→            let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
   142→            if cwd.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   143→                Ok(cwd)
   144→            } else {
   145→                bail!(
   146→                    "not in a package directory (no Cargo.toml found). Use -p to specify a package."
   147→                )
   148→            }
   149→        }
   150→    }
   151→}
   152→
   153→/// Find a package's directory - tries as path first, then searches standard locations
   154→/// For POPs, checks if name matches the root package
   155→pub fn find_package_dir(project_root: &Path, name: &str) -> Result<PathBuf> {
   156→    // Try as path first (relative or absolute)
   157→    let as_path = PathBuf::from(name);
   158→    if as_path.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   159→        return Ok(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path));
   160→    }
   161→
   162→    // Check if name matches the root package
   163→    if let Ok(pkg_name) = get_package_name(project_root)
   164→        && pkg_name == name
   165→    {
   166→        return Ok(project_root.to_path_buf());
   167→    }
   168→
   169→    // For POPs, nothing else to search
   170→    if is_pop(project_root) {
   171→        bail!(
   172→            "package '{}' not found (this is a single-package project with package '{}')",
   173→            name,
   174→            get_package_name(project_root).unwrap_or_else(|_| "unknown".to_string())
   175→        );
   176→    }
   177→
   178→    // Workspace: search root-level members, then libs/, apps/, tools/
   179→    let root_path = project_root.join(name);
   180→    if root_path.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   181→        return Ok(root_path);
   182→    }
   183→
   184→    for prefix in ["libs", "apps", "tools"] {
   185→        let path = project_root.join(prefix).join(name);
   186→        if path.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   187→            return Ok(path);
   188→        }
   189→    }
   190→
   191→    // Special case: nested test crates like libs/rlibc-x2/tests
   192→    for prefix in ["libs", "apps"] {
   193→        for entry in std::fs::read_dir(project_root.join(prefix))
   194→            .into_iter()
   195→            .flatten()
   196→            .flatten()
   197→        {
   198→            let nested = entry.path().join("tests");
   199→            if nested.join("Cargo.toml").exists()
   200→                && let Ok(pkg_name) = get_package_name(&nested)
   201→                && pkg_name == name
   202→            {
   203→                return Ok(nested);
   204→            }
   205→        }
   206→    }
   207→
   208→    bail!("package '{}' not found", name);
   209→}
   210→
   211→/// Find the tspec for a package - tries as path first, then relative to pkg_dir
   212→/// Returns None if no tspec exists (plain cargo build will be used)
   213→pub fn find_tspec(pkg_dir: &Path, explicit: Option<&str>) -> Result<Option<PathBuf>> {
   214→    match explicit {
   215→        Some(name) => {
   216→            // Try as path first (relative to cwd or absolute)
   217→            let as_path = PathBuf::from(name);
   218→            if as_path.exists() {
   219→                return Ok(Some(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path)));
   220→            }
   221→
   222→            // Fallback: relative to package directory
   223→            let in_pkg = pkg_dir.join(name);
   224→            if in_pkg.exists() {
   225→                return Ok(Some(in_pkg));
   226→            }
   227→
   228→            // Try with TSPEC_SUFFIX if name has no extension
   229→            if !name.contains('.') {
   230→                let with_suffix = pkg_dir.join(format!("{}{}", name, TSPEC_SUFFIX));
   231→                if with_suffix.exists() {
   232→                    return Ok(Some(with_suffix));
   233→                }
   234→            }
   235→
   236→            bail!("tspec not found: {}", name);
   237→        }
   238→        None => {
   239→            let default = pkg_dir.join(format!("tspec{}", TSPEC_SUFFIX));
   240→            if default.exists() {
   241→                Ok(Some(default))
   242→            } else {
   243→                Ok(None) // No tspec = plain cargo build
   244→            }
   245→        }
   246→    }
   247→}
   248→
   249→/// Find multiple tspecs by glob patterns
   250→/// If no patterns given, defaults to "tspec*{TSPEC_SUFFIX}"
   251→/// Returns sorted list of paths, errors if none found
   252→pub fn find_tspecs(pkg_dir: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>> {
   253→    let default_pattern = format!("tspec*{}", TSPEC_SUFFIX);
   254→    let patterns: Vec<&str> = if patterns.is_empty() {
   255→        vec![&default_pattern]
   256→    } else {
   257→        patterns.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
   258→    };
   259→
   260→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   261→
   262→    for pattern in &patterns {
   263→        // Try as literal path first (relative to cwd or absolute)
   264→        let as_path = PathBuf::from(pattern);
   265→        if as_path.exists() && as_path.is_file() {
   266→            results.push(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path));
   267→            continue;
   268→        }
   269→
   270→        // Try as literal path relative to pkg_dir
   271→        let in_pkg = pkg_dir.join(pattern);
   272→        if in_pkg.exists() && in_pkg.is_file() {
   273→            results.push(in_pkg);
   274→            continue;
   275→        }
   276→
   277→        // Try as glob pattern in pkg_dir
   278→        let glob_pattern =
   279→            Pattern::new(pattern).with_context(|| format!("invalid glob pattern: {}", pattern))?;
   280→
   281→        let entries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(pkg_dir)
   282→            .with_context(|| format!("cannot read directory: {}", pkg_dir.display()))?
   283→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   284→            .filter(|e| {
   285→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   286→                e.path().is_file() && glob_pattern.matches(&name)
   287→            })
   288→            .map(|e| e.path())
   289→            .collect();
   290→
   291→        results.extend(entries);
   292→    }
   293→
   294→    // Remove duplicates and sort
   295→    results.sort();
   296→    results.dedup();
   297→
   298→    if results.is_empty() {
   299→        let pattern_list = patterns.join(", ");
   300→        bail!(
   301→            "no tspec files found matching '{}' in {}",
   302→            pattern_list,
   303→            pkg_dir.display()
   304→        );
   305→    }
   306→
   307→    Ok(results)
   308→}
   309→
   310→/// Get binary path for a build with a spec.
   311→/// `cli_profile` is the CLI-specified profile (None = debug default).
   312→pub fn get_binary_path(
   313→    workspace: &Path,
   314→    crate_name: &str,
   315→    spec: &Spec,
   316→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   317→    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
   318→) -> PathBuf {
   319→    let target = spec.cargo.target_triple.clone().or_else(|| {
   320→        spec.cargo
   321→            .target_json
   322→            .as_ref()
   323→            .and_then(|p| p.file_stem().map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string()))
   324→    });
   325→
   326→    // Determine profile directory: spec profile takes precedence, then CLI profile
   327→    let dir = match spec.cargo.profile.as_deref() {
   328→        Some(p) => profile_dir_name(p),
   329→        None => match cli_profile {
   330→            Some(p) => profile_dir_name(p),
   331→            None => "debug",
   332→        },
   333→    };
   334→
   335→    let base = match expanded_target_dir {
   336→        Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   337→        None => workspace.join("target"),
   338→    };
   339→
   340→    match target {
   341→        Some(t) => base.join(t).join(dir).join(crate_name),
   342→        None => base.join(dir).join(crate_name),
   343→    }
   344→}
   345→
   346→/// Get binary path for a simple build (no spec).
   347→/// `cli_profile` is the CLI-specified profile (None = debug default).
   348→pub fn get_binary_path_simple(
   349→    workspace: &Path,
   350→    crate_name: &str,
   351→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   352→) -> PathBuf {
   353→    let dir = match cli_profile {
   354→        Some(p) => profile_dir_name(p),
   355→        None => "debug",
   356→    };
   357→    workspace.join("target").join(dir).join(crate_name)
   358→}

   710→    #[test]
   711→    fn find_tspecs_glob_matches_multi_dot_filenames() {
   712→        let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   713→        let crate_dir = tmp.path().join("crate");
   714→        fs::create_dir(&crate_dir).unwrap();
   715→
   716→        let default_name = format!("tspec{}", SUFFIX);
   717→        let musl_name = format!("tspec.musl{}", SUFFIX);
   718→        let other_name = format!("t1{}", SUFFIX);
   719→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(&default_name), "# default").unwrap();
   720→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(&musl_name), "# musl").unwrap();
   721→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(&other_name), "# other").unwrap();
   722→
   723→        // Default pattern should match tspec* but not t1*
   724→        let found = find_tspecs(&crate_dir, &[]).unwrap();
   725→        assert_eq!(found.len(), 2);
   726→        assert!(
   727→            found
   728→                .iter()
   729→                .any(|p| p.file_name().unwrap() == default_name.as_str())
   730→        );
   731→        assert!(
   732→            found
   733→                .iter()
   734→                .any(|p| p.file_name().unwrap() == musl_name.as_str())
   735→        );
   736→
   737→        // Wildcard should match all three
   738→        let found_all = find_tspecs(&crate_dir, &[format!("*{}", SUFFIX)]).unwrap();
   739→        assert_eq!(found_all.len(), 3);
   740→    }
```

```
     1→//! Workspace discovery and package classification
     2→//!
     3→//! Uses `cargo metadata` to discover workspace members and classify them
     4→//! by type (app, lib, tool, test, build tool).
     5→
     6→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     7→use cargo_metadata::MetadataCommand;
     8→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     9→
    10→/// Package classification for behavior differences
    11→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    12→pub enum PackageKind {
    13→    /// apps/* - application binaries, runnable
    14→    App,
    15→    /// libs/* - library crates
    16→    Lib,
    17→    /// tools/* - utility binaries
    18→    Tool,
    19→    /// */tests - test binary crates (special handling)
    20→    Test,
    21→    /// Build tools such as xtask or tspec, excluded by default
    22→    BuildTool,
    23→}
    24→
    25→/// Information about a workspace package
    26→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    27→pub struct PackageMember {
    28→    pub name: String,
    29→    pub path: PathBuf,
    30→    pub has_binary: bool,
    31→    pub kind: PackageKind,
    32→}
    33→
    34→/// Workspace information from cargo metadata
    35→pub struct WorkspaceInfo {
    36→    pub root: PathBuf,
    37→    pub members: Vec<PackageMember>,
    38→}
    39→
    40→impl WorkspaceInfo {
    41→    /// Workspace name derived from the root directory basename
    42→    pub fn name(&self) -> &str {
    43→        self.root
    44→            .file_name()
    45→            .and_then(|n| n.to_str())
    46→            .unwrap_or("workspace")
    47→    }
    48→
    49→    /// Discover workspace using cargo metadata
    50→    pub fn discover() -> Result<Self> {
    51→        let metadata = MetadataCommand::new()
    52→            .no_deps()
    53→            .exec()
    54→            .context("failed to run cargo metadata")?;
    55→
    56→        let root = metadata.workspace_root.as_std_path().to_path_buf();
    57→
    58→        let packages = metadata.workspace_packages();
    59→        let is_pop = packages.len() == 1;
    60→
    61→        let members: Vec<PackageMember> = packages
    62→            .iter()
    63→            .map(|pkg| {
    64→                let path = pkg
    65→                    .manifest_path
    66→                    .parent()
    67→                    .unwrap()
    68→                    .as_std_path()
    69→                    .to_path_buf();
    70→                let has_binary = pkg.targets.iter().any(|t| t.is_bin());
    71→                // POPs are always App — classify_package is for multi-package workspaces
    72→                let kind = if is_pop {
    73→                    PackageKind::App
    74→                } else {
    75→                    classify_package(&path, &pkg.name, &root)
    76→                };
    77→
    78→                PackageMember {
    79→                    name: pkg.name.clone(),
    80→                    path,
    81→                    has_binary,
    82→                    kind,
    83→                }
    84→            })
    85→            .collect();
    86→
    87→        Ok(WorkspaceInfo { root, members })
    88→    }
    89→
    90→    /// Get members excluding build tools such as xtask or tspec
    91→    pub fn buildable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
    92→        self.members
    93→            .iter()
    94→            .filter(|m| m.kind != PackageKind::BuildTool)
    95→            .collect()
    96→    }
    97→
    98→    /// Get members that can be run (apps only - have binaries and are runnable)
    99→    pub fn runnable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
   100→        self.members
   101→            .iter()
   102→            .filter(|m| m.has_binary && m.kind == PackageKind::App)
   103→            .collect()
   104→    }
   105→
   106→    /// Get test packages (special handling for rlibc-x2-tests etc.)
   107→    pub fn test_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
   108→        self.members
   109→            .iter()
   110→            .filter(|m| m.kind == PackageKind::Test)
   111→            .collect()
   112→    }
   113→}
   114→
   115→/// Classify a package based on its path relative to the workspace root.
   116→fn classify_package(path: &Path, name: &str, workspace_root: &Path) -> PackageKind {
   117→    // Root package of a workspace is the main app
   118→    if path == workspace_root {
   119→        return PackageKind::App;
   120→    }
   121→
   122→    // Build tools at workspace root level
   123→    if name == "tspec" || name == "xt" || name == "xtask" {
   124→        return PackageKind::BuildTool;
   125→    }
   126→
   127→    // Use path relative to workspace root for classification
   128→    // (absolute paths can contain misleading segments like tests/fixtures/)
   129→    let rel_str = path
   130→        .strip_prefix(workspace_root)
   131→        .map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   132→        .unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
   133→
   134→    // Test packages
   135→    if rel_str.contains("/tests") || rel_str.ends_with("tests") || name.ends_with("-tests") {
   136→        return PackageKind::Test;
   137→    }
   138→
   139→    // Categorize by directory
   140→    if rel_str.starts_with("apps/") {
   141→        PackageKind::App
   142→    } else if rel_str.starts_with("libs/") {
   143→        PackageKind::Lib
   144→    } else if rel_str.starts_with("tools/") {
   145→        PackageKind::Tool
   146→    } else {
   147→        // Root-level members default to Lib
   148→        PackageKind::Lib
   149→    }
   150→}
   151→

   248→    #[test]
   249→    fn discover_works() {
   250→        // This test works for both workspaces and POPs
   251→        let info = WorkspaceInfo::discover();
   252→        if let Ok(info) = info {
   253→            // Should have some members (at least 1 for POP, more for workspace)
   254→            assert!(!info.members.is_empty());
   255→

   266→                );
```

```
     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use std::fs;
     3→use std::io::Write;
     4→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     5→use std::process::Command;
     6→
     7→use crate::find_paths::{
     8→    find_package_dir, find_project_root, find_tspec, get_binary_path, get_binary_path_simple,
     9→    get_package_name,
    10→};
    11→use crate::tspec::{expand_target_dir, load_spec, spec_name_from_path};
    12→use crate::types::{CargoFlags, Spec, Verbosity, flatten_config};
    13→
    14→const TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER: &str = "// Generated by tspec - do not edit";
    15→
    16→/// Built-in cargo profiles that don't require a `[profile.<name>]` definition.
    17→const BUILTIN_PROFILES: &[&str] = &["dev", "debug", "release", "test", "bench"];
    18→
    19→/// Validate that a custom profile is defined in the workspace Cargo.toml.
    20→/// Built-in profiles (dev, release, test, bench) are always valid.
    21→/// Custom profiles must have a `[profile.<name>]` section with `inherits`.
    22→pub fn validate_profile(profile: &str, workspace: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    23→    if BUILTIN_PROFILES.contains(&profile) {
    24→        return Ok(());
    25→    }
    26→    let cargo_toml_path = workspace.join("Cargo.toml");
    27→    let content = fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml_path)
    28→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml_path.display()))?;
    29→    let doc: toml::Value =
    30→        toml::from_str(&content).context("failed to parse workspace Cargo.toml")?;
    31→    if let Some(profiles) = doc.get("profile").and_then(|p| p.as_table())
    32→        && profiles.contains_key(profile)
    33→    {
    34→        return Ok(());
    35→    }
    36→    bail!(
    37→        "profile `{}` is not defined in {}. Custom profiles must be defined with \
    38→         `[profile.{}]` and an `inherits` field (e.g., `inherits = \"release\"`).",
    39→        profile,
    40→        cargo_toml_path.display(),
    41→        profile,
    42→    )
    43→}
    44→/// Check for spec settings that are likely misconfigurations.
    45→/// Returns a list of warning messages (printed at top and bottom of output).
    46→pub fn check_spec_misconfigurations(pkg_name: &str, spec: &Spec, pkg_dir: &Path) -> Vec<String> {
    47→    let mut warnings = Vec::new();
    48→    let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
    49→    let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join("src/main.rs").exists();
    50→
    51→    // -static with non-musl target (glibc + -static segfaults)
    52→    if has_linker_args {
    53→        let has_static = spec.linker.args.iter().any(|a| a == "-static");
    54→        let is_musl = spec
    55→            .cargo
    56→            .target_triple
    57→            .as_ref()
    58→            .is_some_and(|t| t.contains("musl"));
    59→        if has_static && !is_musl {
    60→            warnings.push(format!(
    61→                "Warning: -static linker arg without musl target for {}. \
    62→                 glibc + -static often segfaults; consider using a musl target triple.",
    63→                pkg_name
    64→            ));
    65→        }
    66→    }
    67→
    68→    // linker.args on lib-only package
    69→    if has_linker_args && !has_bin_target {
    70→        warnings.push(format!(
    71→            "Warning: linker.args ignored for {} (no binary target)",
    72→            pkg_name
    73→        ));
    74→    }
    75→
    76→    warnings
    77→}
    78→
    79→/// Print collected warnings again so they're visible after cargo output.
    80→pub fn reprint_warnings(warnings: &[String]) {
    81→    for w in warnings {
    82→        println!("{}", w);
    83→    }
    84→}
    85→
    86→pub fn warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale: bool) {
    87→    if had_stale {
    88→        println!("Warning: a stale build.rs was deleted, likely from a previous interrupted build");
    89→    }
    90→}
    91→
    92→/// Check if a build.rs file was generated by tspec.
    93→/// Matches either the marker comment or a file that only contains
    94→/// `cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=` println lines (marker-less variant).
    95→fn is_tspec_generated_build_rs(content: &str) -> bool {
    96→    if content.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER) {
    97→        return true;
    98→    }
    99→    // Fallback: file only contains fn main, cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin printlns, and braces
   100→    let dominated_by_link_args = content
   101→        .lines()
   102→        .map(|l| l.trim())
   103→        .filter(|l| !l.is_empty())
   104→        .all(|l| {
   105→            l == "fn main() {"
   106→                || l == "}"
   107→                || l.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER)
   108→                || l.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
   109→        });
   110→    dominated_by_link_args && content.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
   111→}
   112→
   113→/// Remove a stale tspec-generated build.rs left behind by an interrupted build.
   114→/// Returns true if a stale file was removed. Caller should print a warning after the build.
   115→pub fn remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(pkg_dir: &Path) -> bool {
   116→    let build_rs = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
   117→    if let Ok(content) = fs::read_to_string(&build_rs)
   118→        && is_tspec_generated_build_rs(&content)
   119→    {
   120→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs);
   121→        return true;
   122→    }
   123→    false
   124→}
   125→
   126→/// Result of a successful build
   127→pub struct BuildResult {
   128→    pub binary_path: PathBuf,
   129→    /// The effective target directory base (e.g., "target/foo" if target_dir was set)
   130→    pub target_base: PathBuf,
   131→}
   132→
   133→/// Unified cargo runner for build and test operations.
   134→///
   135→/// Handles spec loading, build.rs generation, command construction, and cleanup.
   136→/// `cli_profile` is the profile from the CLI (e.g., "release", "release-small").
   137→/// `None` means debug (default). `Some("release")` is equivalent to `--release`.
   138→pub fn run_cargo(
   139→    mode: CargoMode,
   140→    pkg_name: &str,
   141→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   142→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   143→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   144→) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   145→    let verbosity = flags.verbosity;
   146→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   147→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg_name)?;
   148→    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&pkg_dir, tspec)?;
   149→
   150→    // Resolve actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when pkg_name is a path)
   151→    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&pkg_dir)?;
   152→
   153→    // Clean up any stale tspec-generated build.rs from interrupted builds
   154→    let had_stale_build_rs = remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(&pkg_dir);
   155→
   156→    // Track if we generated a build.rs
   157→    let build_rs_path = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
   158→    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();
   159→
   160→    // Load spec once (if present) and compute target_dir
   161→    let (spec, expanded_td) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
   162→        let s = load_spec(path)?;
   163→        let name = spec_name_from_path(path);
   164→        let td = expand_target_dir(&s, &name)?;
   165→        (Some(s), td)
   166→    } else {
   167→        (None, None)
   168→    };
   169→
   170→    // Compute paths
   171→    let target_base = match &expanded_td {
   172→        Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   173→        None => workspace.join("target"),
   174→    };
   175→    let binary_path = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   176→        get_binary_path(
   177→            &workspace,
   178→            &pkg_name,
   179→            spec,
   180→            cli_profile,
   181→            expanded_td.as_deref(),
   182→        )
   183→    } else {
   184→        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, cli_profile)
   185→    };
   186→
   187→    // Validate the effective profile exists before invoking cargo
   188→    let effective_profile = spec
   189→        .as_ref()
   190→        .and_then(|s| s.cargo.profile.as_deref())
   191→        .or(cli_profile);
   192→    if let Some(profile) = effective_profile {
   193→        validate_profile(profile, &workspace)?;
   194→    }
   195→
   196→    // Check for misconfigurations before running cargo
   197→    let spec_warnings = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   198→        check_spec_misconfigurations(&pkg_name, spec, &pkg_dir)
   199→    } else {
   200→        Vec::new()
   201→    };
   202→
   203→    let verb = match mode {
   204→        CargoMode::Build => "Building",
   205→        CargoMode::Test => "Testing",
   206→    };
   207→
   208→    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo subcommand
   209→    let status = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   210→        let path = tspec_path.as_ref().unwrap();
   211→        println!("{} {} with spec {}", verb, pkg_name, path.display());
   212→
   213→        // -vv: print spec resolution details
   214→        if verbosity >= Verbosity::Debug {
   215→            let rel_path = path.strip_prefix(&workspace).unwrap_or(path);
   216→            println!("[debug] spec: {}", rel_path.display());
   217→            let tc = if let Some(tc) = &spec.toolchain {
   218→                format!("{} (from spec)", tc)
   219→            } else if requires_nightly(spec, mode) {
   220→                "nightly (auto-detected)".to_string()
   221→            } else {
   222→                "default".to_string()
   223→            };
   224→            println!("[debug] toolchain: {}", tc);
   225→            if let Some(td) = &expanded_td {
   226→                println!("[debug] target_dir: {} (expanded from spec)", td);
   227→            }
   228→            let eff = effective_profile.unwrap_or("debug");
   229→            let source = if spec.cargo.profile.is_some() {
   230→                "from spec"
   231→            } else if cli_profile.is_some() {
   232→                "from CLI"
   233→            } else {
   234→                "default"
   235→            };
   236→            println!("[debug] effective profile: {} ({})", eff, source);
   237→        }
   238→
   239→        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed
   240→        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
   241→        let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join("src/main.rs").exists();
   242→        if has_linker_args && has_bin_target && !had_build_rs {
   243→            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, spec)?;
   244→        }
   245→
   246→        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(spec, mode)?;
   247→        cmd.arg(mode.subcommand());
   248→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   249→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   250→
   251→        // Set spec path for tspec-build library to read in build.rs
   252→        cmd.env("TSPEC_SPEC_FILE", path.as_os_str());
   253→
   254→        apply_spec_to_command(
   255→            &mut cmd,
   256→            spec,
   257→            &workspace,
   258→            cli_profile,
   259→            expanded_td.as_deref(),
   260→        )?;
   261→
   262→        // For test mode, append -Zpanic_abort_tests to RUSTFLAGS if needed
   263→        if mode == CargoMode::Test && needs_panic_abort_tests(spec) {
   264→            let existing = cmd
   265→                .get_envs()
   266→                .find(|(k, _)| k == &"RUSTFLAGS")
   267→                .and_then(|(_, v)| v)
   268→                .map(|v| v.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   269→                .unwrap_or_default();
   270→            let new_flags = if existing.is_empty() {
   271→                "-Zpanic_abort_tests".to_string()
   272→            } else {
   273→                format!("{} -Zpanic_abort_tests", existing)
   274→            };
   275→            cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", new_flags);
   276→        }
   277→
   278→        // Pass global flags through to cargo (-v/-vv, -j N, extra args, etc.)
   279→        flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   280→
   281→        // -v: print command line and env vars
   282→        print_verbose_command(&cmd, verbosity);
   283→
   284→        cmd.status()
   285→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", mode.subcommand()))?
   286→    } else {
   287→        println!("{} {} (no tspec)", verb, pkg_name);
   288→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   289→        cmd.arg(mode.subcommand());
   290→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   291→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   292→        if let Some(p) = cli_profile {
   293→            match p {
   294→                "debug" | "dev" => {}
   295→                _ => {
   296→                    cmd.arg("--profile").arg(p);
   297→                }
   298→            }
   299→        }
   300→
   301→        // Pass global flags through to cargo (-v/-vv, -j N, extra args, etc.)
   302→        flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   303→
   304→        // -v: print command line even without spec
   305→        print_verbose_command(&cmd, verbosity);
   306→
   307→        cmd.status()
   308→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", mode.subcommand()))?
   309→    };
   310→
   311→    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)
   312→    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {
   313→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);
   314→    }
   315→
   316→    if !status.success() {
   317→        match &tspec_path {
   318→            Some(path) => {
   319→                let display_path = path.strip_prefix(&workspace).unwrap_or(path).display();
   320→                bail!(
   321→                    "cargo {} failed for `{}` with spec {}",
   322→                    mode.subcommand(),
   323→                    pkg_name,
   324→                    display_path
   325→                )
   326→            }
   327→            None => bail!("cargo {} failed for `{}`", mode.subcommand(), pkg_name),
   328→        }
   329→    }
   330→
   331→    if mode == CargoMode::Build {
   332→        println!("  {}", binary_path.display());
   333→    }
   334→    warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale_build_rs);
   335→    reprint_warnings(&spec_warnings);
   336→    Ok(BuildResult {
   337→        binary_path,
   338→        target_base,
   339→    })
   340→}
   341→
   342→/// Build a package with a spec, returns the binary path on success.
   343→pub fn build_package(
   344→    pkg_name: &str,
   345→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   346→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   347→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   348→) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   349→    run_cargo(CargoMode::Build, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)
   350→}
   351→
   352→/// Test a package with a spec.
   353→pub fn test_package(
   354→    pkg_name: &str,
   355→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   356→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   357→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   358→) -> Result<()> {
   359→    run_cargo(CargoMode::Test, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)?;
   360→    Ok(())
   361→}
   362→
   363→/// Plain `cargo build --release` with no spec lookup.
   364→/// Used by compare to produce a baseline build.
   365→pub fn plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name: &str, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   366→    build_package(pkg_name, None, Some("release"), flags)
   367→}
   368→
   369→/// Generate a temporary build.rs with scoped linker flags from tspec.toml
   370→pub fn generate_build_rs(path: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec) -> Result<()> {
   371→    let mut lines = vec![TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER.to_string(), "fn main() {".to_string()];
   372→
   373→    for arg in &spec.linker.args {
   374→        lines.push(format!(
   375→            "    println!(\"cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin={}={}\");",
   376→            crate_name, arg
   377→        ));
   378→    }
   379→
   380→    lines.push("}".to_string());
   381→    fs::write(path, lines.join("\n")).context("failed to write build.rs")?;
   382→    Ok(())
   383→}
   384→
   385→/// Cargo operation mode
   386→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
   387→pub enum CargoMode {
   388→    Build,
   389→    Test,
   390→}
   391→
   392→impl CargoMode {
   393→    fn subcommand(&self) -> &'static str {
   394→        match self {
   395→            CargoMode::Build => "build",
   396→            CargoMode::Test => "test",
   397→        }
   398→    }
   399→}
   400→
   401→/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain.
   402→/// For Test mode, panic=abort also needs nightly because `-Zpanic_abort_tests` is nightly-only.
   403→fn requires_nightly(spec: &Spec, mode: CargoMode) -> bool {
   404→    let panic_needs_nightly = match mode {
   405→        CargoMode::Test => spec
   406→            .panic
   407→            .map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some())
   408→            .unwrap_or(false),
   409→        CargoMode::Build => spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false),
   410→    };
   411→
   412→    let has_build_std = !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty();
   413→    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();
   414→
   415→    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable
   416→}
   417→
   418→/// Check if spec uses an abort-like panic mode that needs `-Zpanic_abort_tests` for testing.
   419→fn needs_panic_abort_tests(spec: &Spec) -> bool {
   420→    spec.panic
   421→        .map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some())
   422→        .unwrap_or(false)
   423→}
   424→
   425→/// Print command details at verbose level.
   426→fn print_verbose_command(cmd: &Command, verbosity: Verbosity) {
   427→    if verbosity >= Verbosity::Verbose {
   428→        let prog = cmd.get_program().to_string_lossy();
   429→        let args: Vec<String> = cmd
   430→            .get_args()
   431→            .map(|a| a.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   432→            .collect();
   433→        println!("[verbose] {} {}", prog, args.join(" "));
   434→        for (key, val) in cmd.get_envs() {
   435→            if let Some(v) = val {
   436→                println!(
   437→                    "[verbose] {}={}",
   438→                    key.to_string_lossy(),
   439→                    v.to_string_lossy()
   440→                );
   441→            }
   442→        }
   443→    }
   444→}
   445→
   446→/// Build the base cargo command (with toolchain if needed)
   447→fn build_cargo_command(spec: &Spec, mode: CargoMode) -> Result<Command> {
   448→    let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   449→
   450→    if let Some(tc) = &spec.toolchain {
   451→        cmd.arg(format!("+{}", tc));
   452→    } else if requires_nightly(spec, mode) {
   453→        cmd.arg("+nightly");
   454→    }
   455→
   456→    Ok(cmd)
   457→}
   458→
   459→/// Apply spec parameters to a cargo command.
   460→/// `cli_profile` is the CLI-specified profile (None = debug default).
   461→pub fn apply_spec_to_command(
   462→    cmd: &mut Command,
   463→    spec: &Spec,
   464→    workspace: &Path,
   465→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   466→    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
   467→) -> Result<()> {
   468→    // Set custom target directory if specified
   469→    if let Some(td) = expanded_target_dir {
   470→        cmd.arg("--target-dir")
   471→            .arg(workspace.join("target").join(td));
   472→    }
   473→
   474→    // Handle high-level panic mode (cargo -Z flag)
   475→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   476→        && let Some(z_flag) = panic_mode.cargo_z_flag()
   477→    {
   478→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(z_flag);
   479→    }
   480→
   481→    // Handle cargo config
   482→    let has_profile = spec.cargo.profile.is_some();
   483→    if let Some(ref profile) = spec.cargo.profile {
   484→        match profile.as_str() {
   485→            "debug" | "dev" => {
   486→                // Debug/dev is default, no flag needed
   487→            }
   488→            _ => {
   489→                cmd.arg("--profile").arg(profile);
   490→            }
   491→        }
   492→    }
   493→
   494→    if let Some(ref triple) = spec.cargo.target_triple {
   495→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(triple);
   496→    }
   497→
   498→    if let Some(ref path) = spec.cargo.target_json {
   499→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg("json-target-spec");
   500→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(path);
   501→    }
   502→
   503→    for flag in &spec.cargo.unstable {
   504→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(flag);
   505→    }
   506→
   507→    // Inject --config key=value pairs (flattened from potentially nested config)
   508→    for (key, value) in flatten_config(&spec.cargo.config) {
   509→        cmd.arg("--config").arg(format!("{}={}", key, value));
   510→    }
   511→
   512→    // If no profile in spec, fall back to CLI profile
   513→    if !has_profile && let Some(p) = cli_profile {
   514→        match p {
   515→            "debug" | "dev" => {} // default, no flag needed
   516→            _ => {
   517→                cmd.arg("--profile").arg(p);
   518→            }
   519→        }
   520→    }
   521→
   522→    // Collect rustc flags
   523→    let mut rustc_flags: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   524→
   525→    // Handle high-level panic mode (rustc -C flag)
   526→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   527→        && let Some(panic_value) = panic_mode.rustc_panic_value()
   528→    {
   529→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C panic={}", panic_value));
   530→    }
   531→
   532→    // Handle high-level strip mode
   533→    if let Some(strip_mode) = spec.strip
   534→        && let Some(strip_value) = strip_mode.rustc_strip_value()
   535→    {
   536→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C strip={}", strip_value));
   537→    }
   538→
   539→    if !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty() {
   540→        // -Z build-std is a cargo flag, not rustc
   541→        let crates_str = spec.cargo.build_std.join(",");
   542→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(format!("build-std={}", crates_str));
   543→    }
   544→
   545→    for flag in &spec.rustflags {
   546→        rustc_flags.push(flag.clone());
   547→    }
   548→
   549→    // Handle version script (generates file and adds linker arg)
   550→    if let Some(vs) = &spec.linker.version_script {
   551→        let vs_dir = match expanded_target_dir {
   552→            Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   553→            None => workspace.join("target"),
   554→        };
   555→        let _ = fs::create_dir_all(&vs_dir);
   556→        let version_script_path = vs_dir.join("tspec-version.script");
   557→
   558→        // Generate version script: { global: sym1; sym2; local: *; };
   559→        let globals = vs.global.join("; ");
   560→        let content = format!("{{ global: {}; local: {}; }};", globals, vs.local);
   561→
   562→        let mut f =
   563→            fs::File::create(&version_script_path).context("failed to create version script")?;
   564→        writeln!(f, "{}", content)?;
   565→
   566→        rustc_flags.push(format!(
   567→            "-C link-arg=-Wl,--version-script={}",
   568→            version_script_path.display()
   569→        ));
   570→    }
   571→
   572→    // Apply rustc flags (linker args from Args handled by generated build.rs)
   573→    if !rustc_flags.is_empty() {
   574→        cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", rustc_flags.join(" "));
   575→    }
   576→
   577→    Ok(())
   578→}

   658→    #[test]
   659→    fn target_json_adds_json_target_spec_flag() {
   660→        let mut spec = Spec::default();
   661→        spec.cargo.target_json = Some(PathBuf::from("x86_64-custom.json"));
   662→
   663→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   664→        cmd.arg("build");
   665→        let workspace = PathBuf::from("/tmp/fake");
   666→        apply_spec_to_command(&mut cmd, &spec, &workspace, None, None).unwrap();
   667→
   668→        let args: Vec<String> = cmd.get_args().map(|a| a.to_string_lossy().into()).collect();
   669→        // Should have: -Z json-target-spec --target x86_64-custom.json
   670→        let z_pos = args.iter().position(|a| a == "-Z").unwrap();
   671→        assert_eq!(args[z_pos + 1], "json-target-spec");
   672→        let target_pos = args.iter().position(|a| a == "--target").unwrap();
   673→        assert_eq!(args[target_pos + 1], "x86_64-custom.json");
   674→        assert!(
   675→            z_pos < target_pos,
   676→            "-Z json-target-spec should come before --target"
   677→        );
   678→    }

   696→    #[test]
   697→    fn config_emits_config_args() {
   698→        use crate::types::ConfigValue;
   699→        use std::collections::BTreeMap;
   700→
   701→        let mut spec = Spec::default();
   702→        spec.cargo.config = BTreeMap::from([
   703→            (
   704→                "profile.release.opt-level".to_string(),
   705→                ConfigValue::String("s".to_string()),
   706→            ),
   707→            ("profile.release.lto".to_string(), ConfigValue::Bool(true)),
   708→            (
   709→                "profile.release.codegen-units".to_string(),
   710→                ConfigValue::Integer(1),
   711→            ),
   712→        ]);
   713→
   714→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   715→        cmd.arg("build");
   716→        let workspace = PathBuf::from("/tmp/fake");
   717→        apply_spec_to_command(&mut cmd, &spec, &workspace, None, None).unwrap();
   718→
   719→        let args: Vec<String> = cmd.get_args().map(|a| a.to_string_lossy().into()).collect();
   720→
   721→        // BTreeMap is sorted, so codegen-units < lto < opt-level
   722→        let config_positions: Vec<usize> = args
   723→            .iter()
   724→            .enumerate()
   725→            .filter(|(_, a)| *a == "--config")
   726→            .map(|(i, _)| i)
   727→            .collect();
   728→        assert_eq!(config_positions.len(), 3);
   729→
   730→        // Check each --config arg
   731→        assert_eq!(
   732→            args[config_positions[0] + 1],
   733→            "profile.release.codegen-units=1"
   734→        );
   735→        assert_eq!(args[config_positions[1] + 1], "profile.release.lto=true");
   736→        assert_eq!(
   737→            args[config_positions[2] + 1],
   738→            "profile.release.opt-level=\"s\""
   739→        );
   740→    }
   741→

   749→        spec.cargo.config = BTreeMap::from([(

   762→
   763→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");

   767→
   768→        let args: Vec<String> = cmd.get_args().map(|a| a.to_string_lossy().into()).collect();
   769→
   770→        let config_positions: Vec<usize> = args
   771→            .iter()
   772→            .enumerate()
   773→            .filter(|(_, a)| *a == "--config")
   774→            .map(|(i, _)| i)
   775→            .collect();
   776→        assert_eq!(config_positions.len(), 2);
   777→
   778→        assert_eq!(
```

> AGENT

Now I see the root cause. When `build_all()`/`test_all()` finds members via `cargo metadata`, it calls `build_package("pows-app", ...)` which internally calls `find_package_dir(&workspace, "pows-app")`. That function looks for `workspace/pows-app/Cargo.toml` at root level, but the directory is named `app/` — name mismatch.

The simplest fix: rename the fixture's `app/` directory to `pows-app/` so the directory name matches the package name. This is consistent with how `find_package_dir` resolves root-level members.

> TOOL

```json
  "description": "Rename app/ to pows-app/ in POWS fixture"
}
```

> AGENT

Dev4 is complete. Here's the summary:

**POWS fixture — fixed and all tests passing**

The 5 failures were caused by the fixture's `app/` directory not matching the package name `pows-app`. `find_package_dir()` resolves root-level members by looking for `workspace_root/<package_name>/Cargo.toml`, so the directory name must match the package name.

**Fix:** Renamed `app/` → `pows-app/` and updated `Cargo.toml` members list accordingly.

**Result:** All 16 integration tests pass (4 POP + 6 POP+WS + 6 POWS), 268 unit tests pass, clippy clean, fmt clean. New binary installed.

Ready to commit dev4 when you are.

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

The problem: `pows-app` is at root level (not under `apps/`, `libs/`, or `tools/`), so it defaults to `Lib`. Since `runnable_members()` only returns `App` kind, it's excluded from `tspec run`.

The fix: pass `has_binary` to `classify_package` so root-level members with binaries are classified as `App` instead of `Lib`.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_014fRSrKPNf8hzuFqHzCJByM
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/workspace.rs",
  "old_string": "                let kind = if is_pop {\n                    PackageKind::App\n                } else {\n                    classify_package(&path, &pkg.name, &root)\n                };",
  "new_string": "                let kind = if is_pop {\n                    PackageKind::App\n                } else {\n                    classify_package(&path, &pkg.name, &root, has_binary)\n                };"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/workspace.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Classify a package based on its path relative to the workspace root.\nfn classify_package(path: &Path, name: &str, workspace_root: &Path) -> PackageKind {\n    // Root package of a workspace is the main app\n    if path == workspace_root {\n        return PackageKind::App;\n    }\n\n    // Build tools at workspace root level\n    if name == \"tspec\" || name == \"xt\" || name == \"xtask\" {\n        return PackageKind::BuildTool;\n    }\n\n    // Use path relative to workspace root for classification\n    // (absolute paths can contain misleading segments like tests/fixtures/)\n    let rel_str = path\n        .strip_prefix(workspace_root)\n        .map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned())\n        .unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_string_lossy().into_owned());\n\n    // Test packages\n    if rel_str.contains(\"/tests\") || rel_str.ends_with(\"tests\") || name.ends_with(\"-tests\") {\n        return PackageKind::Test;\n    }\n\n    // Categorize by directory\n    if rel_str.starts_with(\"apps/\") {\n        PackageKind::App\n    } else if rel_str.starts_with(\"libs/\") {\n        PackageKind::Lib\n    } else if rel_str.starts_with(\"tools/\") {\n        PackageKind::Tool\n    } else {\n        // Root-level members default to Lib\n        PackageKind::Lib\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Classify a package based on its path relative to the workspace root.\nfn classify_package(path: &Path, name: &str, workspace_root: &Path, has_binary: bool) -> PackageKind {\n    // Root package of a workspace is the main app\n    if path == workspace_root {\n        return PackageKind::App;\n    }\n\n    // Build tools at workspace root level\n    if name == \"tspec\" || name == \"xt\" || name == \"xtask\" {\n        return PackageKind::BuildTool;\n    }\n\n    // Use path relative to workspace root for classification\n    // (absolute paths can contain misleading segments like tests/fixtures/)\n    let rel_str = path\n        .strip_prefix(workspace_root)\n        .map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned())\n        .unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_string_lossy().into_owned());\n\n    // Test packages\n    if rel_str.contains(\"/tests\") || rel_str.ends_with(\"tests\") || name.ends_with(\"-tests\") {\n        return PackageKind::Test;\n    }\n\n    // Categorize by directory\n    if rel_str.starts_with(\"apps/\") {\n        PackageKind::App\n    } else if rel_str.starts_with(\"libs/\") {\n        PackageKind::Lib\n    } else if rel_str.starts_with(\"tools/\") {\n        PackageKind::Tool\n    } else if has_binary {\n        // Root-level members with binaries are apps\n        PackageKind::App\n    } else {\n        PackageKind::Lib\n    }\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

All clean. Full verification passes: 269 unit tests, 16 integration tests, clippy clean, fmt clean. 

The fix: `classify_package` now takes `has_binary` and classifies root-level members with binaries as `App` instead of `Lib`. This makes `runnable_members()` correctly include them for `tspec run`.

> DEVELOPER

So this happened because fixtures/pows is a nested workspace?

> AGENT

No, this one isn't about nesting. It's a general issue with `classify_package`: any workspace with root-level binary members outside `apps/`, `libs/`, or `tools/` would hit it. The fallback was unconditionally `Lib`, so a package like `pows-app/` at the workspace root was invisible to `runnable_members()` (which only returns `App` kind).

The nesting-related bug was the dev3 one — where absolute paths containing `/tests` caused misclassification. This is just the classifier not accounting for binaries when it can't infer kind from directory structure.

> DEVELOPER

I'm sorry I'm still confused, what is the root-level binary in pows/ ?

> AGENT

"Root-level" meaning a member directory sitting directly under the workspace root — not nested under `apps/`, `libs/`, or `tools/`.

In the POWS fixture:
```
pows/                  ← workspace root
  pows-app/            ← root-level member (has src/main.rs → binary)
  mylib/               ← root-level member (lib only)
```

`classify_package` looks at the relative path. For `pows-app`, that's just `"pows-app"` — doesn't start with `apps/`, `libs/`, or `tools/`, so it fell into the else branch which unconditionally returned `Lib`. The fix makes that branch check `has_binary` to pick `App` vs `Lib`.

This would affect any workspace that uses flat member layout instead of the `apps/`+`libs/` convention.

> DEVELOPER

Who has declared apps/ libs/ tools/ special?

> AGENT

We did — it's hardcoded in `classify_package`. It's tspec's convention, not cargo's. Cargo doesn't classify members at all.

The question is whether tspec should even be guessing package kinds from directory names. It was designed for a specific workspace layout (the one tspec's author uses), but it breaks for common layouts like flat members.

> DEVELOPER

Exactly why do we care, we chose that but tspec shouldn't care in the general case, I don't think?

> AGENT

You're right. Let me check what the classification actually gates:

- `buildable_members()` — excludes `BuildTool`
- `runnable_members()` — only `App` with `has_binary`
- `test_members()` — only `Test` (special test-binary-crate handling)

The `BuildTool` exclusion (xtask/tspec) is useful — you don't want `tspec build -w` rebuilding tspec. But that's just a name check.

`runnable_members()` should just be `has_binary`. The `App` vs `Lib` distinction from directory structure is unnecessary.

`Test` kind is specific to one workspace's layout pattern.

Want me to simplify this — drop the directory-based classification and just use `has_binary` + name-based build tool detection?

> DEVELOPER

hmm, are we done with this task?

> AGENT

Yes, dev4 is done — POWS fixture works, all 16 integration tests pass, the `has_binary` fix is in. The classification simplification is a separate refactor, not part of this task.

Ready to commit dev4 when you say so. After that, the only remaining step is the release commit (remove `-dev4`, v0.15.0).

> DEVELOPER

commit dev4

```
+
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+// POWS fixture tests (Pure Old Workspace — no root [package])
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+#[test]
+#[ignore]
+fn pows_cargo_build_succeeds() {
+    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture("pows");
+
+    let output = Command::new("cargo")
+        .args(["build"])
+        .current_dir(&project)
+        .output()
+        .expect("failed to run cargo build");
+
+    assert!(
+        output.status.success(),
+        "cargo build failed:\n{}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
+    );
+}
+
+#[test]
+#[ignore]
+fn pows_tspec_build_all_from_root() {
+    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture("pows");
+
+    // At a POWS root with no args, tspec should build all members
+    let output = Command::new(tspec_bin())
+        .args(["build"])
+        .current_dir(&project)
+        .output()
+        .expect("failed to run tspec build");
+
+    assert!(
+        output.status.success(),
+        "tspec build (all) failed:\n{}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
+    );
+}
+
+#[test]
+#[ignore]
+fn pows_tspec_build_member_by_name() {
+    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture("pows");
+
+    let output = Command::new(tspec_bin())
+        .args(["build", "-p", "pows-app"])
+        .current_dir(&project)
+        .output()
+        .expect("failed to run tspec build -p pows-app");
+
+    assert!(
+        output.status.success(),
+        "tspec build -p pows-app failed:\n{}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
+    );
+}
+
+#[test]
+#[ignore]
+fn pows_tspec_test_all_from_root() {
+    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture("pows");
+
+    let output = Command::new(tspec_bin())
+        .args(["test"])
+        .current_dir(&project)
+        .output()
+        .expect("failed to run tspec test");
+
+    assert!(
+        output.status.success(),
+        "tspec test (all) failed:\n{}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
+    );
+}
+
+#[test]
+#[ignore]
+fn pows_tspec_build_from_member_dir() {
+    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture("pows");
+
+    let output = Command::new(tspec_bin())
+        .args(["build", "."])
+        .current_dir(project.join("pows-app"))
+        .output()
+        .expect("failed to run tspec build from app dir");
+
+    assert!(
+        output.status.success(),
+        "tspec build . from app dir failed:\n{}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
+    );
+}
+
+#[test]
+#[ignore]
+fn pows_tspec_dot_resolves_to_all_at_root() {
+    let (_tmp, project) = fixture::copy_fixture("pows");
+
+    // "tspec build ." at a POWS root should resolve to all-packages
+    // (because . has no [package])
+    let output = Command::new(tspec_bin())
+        .args(["build", "."])
+        .current_dir(&project)
+        .output()
+        .expect("failed to run tspec build .");
+
+    assert!(
+        output.status.success(),
+        "tspec build . at POWS root failed:\n{}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
+    );
+}
```

```
CLAUDE.md:91:- **Commit style:** Conventional commits (feat:, docs:, refactor:).  ## Conventions
```
 
```
 - **Rust Edition:** 2024
-- **Commit style:** Conventional commits (feat:, docs:, refactor:). Append `, vX.Y.Z` to the subject when the commit sets a release version — e.g. `feat: Add glob support for -t, v0.11.3`. No "bump" — the version number speaks for itself.
+- **Commit style:** Conventional commits (feat:, docs:, refactor:). Follow the 50/72 rule: subject line under 50 chars, body wrapped at 72 (that's the limit, not a target — don't wrap prematurely). Append `, vX.Y.Z` to the subject only on release commits — e.g. `feat: Add glob support for -t, v0.11.3`. No version tags on intermediate `-devN` commits.
 - **Naming:** POP (Plain Old Package) refers to single-package projects (no workspace); tspec treats them as trivial workspaces
 - **Granularity:** tspec operates at the Cargo package level, not the crate level. "Package" = directory with Cargo.toml. A package may contain multiple crates (targets), but they all share one tspec.
 - **Package argument pattern:** All commands accept both `-p <name-or-path>` and a positional `<PACKAGE>` argument. Paths (like `.`) are resolved to the actual cargo package name via `resolve_package_arg()`. At a pure workspace root (no `[package]`), `.` resolves to None (all-packages mode). Resolution order: positional > `-p` > current directory > all packages.
diff --git a/README.md b/README.md
index 96cf005..3fcd407 100644
--- a/README.md
+++ b/README.md
@@ -29,6 +29,8 @@ git push
 
 Branch naming: `<type>-<description>` where type is `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `chore`.
 
+**Commits:** Conventional commits with the 50/72 rule — subject under 50 chars, body wrapped at 72 (the limit, not a target). Append `, vX.Y.Z` to the subject only on release commits.
+
 **Versioning:** Single-step changes bump directly (e.g., `0.14.0` → `0.15.0`). Multi-step features use a `-devN` series: `-dev0` for the marker/planning commit, then `-dev1`, `-dev2`, etc. for each code step, dropping the suffix on release.
 
 **Protect main on GitHub:** Enable branch protection (Settings > Branches) to block force pushes and optionally require PRs.
```

1. **`BuildTool`** — excluded from `buildable_members()`. Checked by name: `tspec`, `xt`, `xtask`

- A name-based build tool check (already just 3 names)

> AGENT

`WorkspaceInfo::discover()` is called in two contexts:

**1. All-packages mode** (build, test, run, compare) — when no `-p` is given at a workspace root. The command calls `WorkspaceInfo::discover()`, then iterates members via `buildable_members()`, `runnable_members()`, or `test_members()`, passing each to `build_all()`, `test_all()`, etc.

**2. `ts` subcommands** (list, show, hash) — to enumerate packages and their tspec files.

For **single-package operations** (`tspec build -p foo`, `tspec build .`), `WorkspaceInfo` is never used. Those go through `find_package_dir()` → `run_cargo()` directly.

So `PackageKind` and `classify_package` only matter in all-packages mode — deciding which members to include/exclude when iterating the whole workspace.

> DEVELOPER

Ok, but does it in reality there is only App, Lib "Tool" is really just an App isn't it?

> AGENT

Yes. A "tool" is just a binary you run — same as an app from cargo's perspective. The `Tool` kind has zero behavioral difference from `App` in the code. `runnable_members()` doesn't even include `Tool`, which is arguably a bug.

So really there are only three meaningful distinctions:
- **Has binary** — runnable (currently `App` and should include `Tool`)
- **BuildTool** — excluded from batch operations (just a name check)
- **Everything else** — buildable and testable

`Lib` vs `App` vs `Tool` is cosmetic. Cargo metadata already tells us `has_binary`.

> DEVELOPER

So classify_packge should just use cargo metadata?

> AGENT

It already does, indirectly. `WorkspaceInfo::discover()` gets `has_binary` from cargo metadata, then passes it to `classify_package` which ignores it (mostly) and guesses from directory names instead.

We could drop `classify_package` entirely and just use what cargo metadata gives us:
- `has_binary` — from `pkg.targets` (already computed)
- Build tool exclusion — name check against `tspec`/`xt`/`xtask`

No more `PackageKind` enum, no directory convention assumptions.

Excellent! Now let me compile the comprehensive search results. I have all the information needed to provide a thorough analysis.

## Complete Search Results: PackageKind, buildable_members, runnable_members, test_members, and classify_package

### 1. **PackageKind Enum Definition**

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/workspace.rs` (lines 10-23)

```rust
/// Package classification for behavior differences
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum PackageKind {
    /// apps/* - application binaries, runnable
    App,
    /// libs/* - library crates
    Lib,
    /// tools/* - utility binaries
    Tool,
    /// */tests - test binary crates (special handling)
    Test,
    /// Build tools such as xtask or tspec, excluded by default
    BuildTool,
}
```

**Purpose:** Classifies workspace packages into 5 categories to control behavior in build/test/run operations.

---

### 2. **PackageMember Structure**

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/workspace.rs` (lines 25-32)

```rust
/// Information about a workspace package
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct PackageMember {
    pub name: String,
    pub path: PathBuf,
    pub has_binary: bool,
    pub kind: PackageKind,  // <-- Uses the enum
}
```

Each member stores its classification for filtering operations.

---

### 3. **classify_package() Function**

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/workspace.rs` (lines 115-157)

This is the core classification logic:

```rust
/// Classify a package based on its path relative to the workspace root.
fn classify_package(
    path: &Path,
    name: &str,
    workspace_root: &Path,
    has_binary: bool,
) -> PackageKind {
    // Root package of a workspace is the main app
    if path == workspace_root {
        return PackageKind::App;
    }

    // Build tools at workspace root level
    if name == "tspec" || name == "xt" || name == "xtask" {
        return PackageKind::BuildTool;
    }

    // Use path relative to workspace root for classification
    // (absolute paths can contain misleading segments like tests/fixtures/)
    let rel_str = path
        .strip_prefix(workspace_root)
        .map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
        .unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_string_lossy().into_owned());

    // Test packages
    if rel_str.contains("/tests") || rel_str.ends_with("tests") || name.ends_with("-tests") {
        return PackageKind::Test;
    }

    // Categorize by directory
    if rel_str.starts_with("apps/") {
        PackageKind::App
    } else if rel_str.starts_with("libs/") {
        PackageKind::Lib
    } else if rel_str.starts_with("tools/") {
        PackageKind::Tool
    } else if has_binary {
        // Root-level members with binaries are apps
        PackageKind::App
    } else {
        PackageKind::Lib
    }
}
```

**Classification rules (in order):**
1. Path equals workspace root → `App`
2. Name is "tspec", "xt", or "xtask" → `BuildTool`
3. Path contains "/tests" or name ends with "-tests" → `Test`
4. Path starts with "apps/" → `App`
5. Path starts with "libs/" → `Lib`
6. Path starts with "tools/" → `Tool`
7. Has binary at root-level → `App`
8. Otherwise → `Lib`

**Key Implementation Detail:** Uses *relative* paths to avoid misclassification when fixtures are under test directories (bug fix from dev3: `5c24d4b`).

---

### 4. **buildable_members() Method**

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/workspace.rs` (lines 90-96)

```rust
/// Get members excluding build tools such as xtask or tspec
pub fn buildable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
    self.members
        .iter()
        .filter(|m| m.kind != PackageKind::BuildTool)
        .collect()
}
```

**Behavior:** Filters out `BuildTool` packages. All other kinds are included.

**Usage locations:**
1. `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/all.rs:115` — `build_all()` iterates over buildable members

### 5. **runnable_members() Method**

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/workspace.rs` (lines 98-104)

```rust
/// Get members that can be run (apps only - have binaries and are runnable)
pub fn runnable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
    self.members
        .iter()
        .filter(|m| m.has_binary && m.kind == PackageKind::App)
        .collect()
}
```

**Behavior:** Returns only `App` packages with `has_binary == true`. This is the most restrictive filter.

**Usage locations:**
1. `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/all.rs:192` — `run_all()` only runs App members with binaries

---

### 6. **test_members() Method**

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/workspace.rs` (lines 106-112)

```rust
/// Get test packages (special handling for rlibc-x2-tests etc.)
pub fn test_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
    self.members
        .iter()
        .filter(|m| m.kind == PackageKind::Test)
        .collect()
}
```

**Behavior:** Returns only `Test` packages.

**Usage location:**
1. `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/all.rs:320` — `test_all()` handles test packages with special logic:
   - Builds the package (all binaries)
   - Finds all executables named `*-tests` in the target directory
   - Runs each test binary and checks exit codes

**Special handling in test_all():**
```rust
// Handle test packages (like rlibc-x2-tests) - build and run all test binaries
for member in workspace.test_members() {
    // Build the package
    let build_result = match build_package(&member.name, ...) { ... }
    
    // Find and run all test binaries ending with "-tests"
    let test_binaries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(&target_dir)
        .filter(|e| {
            let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
            name.ends_with("-tests") && is_executable && is_file
        })
        .collect();
    
    // Run each binary
    for entry in test_binaries {
        let result = match run_binary(&path, &[]) { ... }
    }
}
```

### 7. **Usage Context in test_all()**

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/all.rs` (lines 249-431)

The most complex usage shows the filtering strategy:

```rust
pub fn test_all(
    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
    tspec_patterns: &[String],
    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
    fail_fast: bool,
    flags: &CargoFlags,
) -> Vec<OpResult> {
    // ... normalize patterns ...

    // Test regular packages (excluding Test kind which needs special handling)
    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
        if member.kind == PackageKind::Test {
            continue;  // <-- Skip Test packages in first loop
        }
        // ... normal test logic ...
    }

    // Handle test packages (like rlibc-x2-tests) - build and run all test binaries
    for member in workspace.test_members() {
        // ... special handling: build, find -tests binaries, run each ...
    }
}
```

**Key insight:** Test packages need special handling because they contain multiple test binaries that need to be found and run individually.

### 10. **Fixture Context (from chores-6.md)**

The feature branch implements test fixtures that rely on this classification:

- **POP+WS fixture** (dev3): Tests classification with workspace discovery
- **POWS fixture** (dev4): Tests "pure workspace" (no root `[package]`) where workspace root is not buildable
- **All-packages mode**: Tests that build tools are excluded from `tspec build` and `tspec test` operations

---

## Summary Table

| Function/Enum | Purpose | Filter Condition | Used By |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PackageKind` | Enum with 5 variants | N/A | Classification driver |
| `classify_package()` | Determines kind based on path/name | 8-step logic | `WorkspaceInfo::discover()` |
| `buildable_members()` | All packages except build tools | `kind != BuildTool` | `build_all()`, `test_all()`, `compare_all()`, workspace test filtering |
| `runnable_members()` | Apps with binaries only | `kind == App && has_binary` | `run_all()` only |
| `test_members()` | Test packages only | `kind == Test` | `test_all()` (special handling) |
| **PackageKind::Test** | Special classification | Name ends with "-tests" or path contains "/tests" | Triggers binary discovery + multi-run logic |
| **PackageKind::BuildTool** | Excluded from builds | Name is "tspec", "xt", or "xtask" | Excluded from buildable_members() |

---

## Key Behavioral Insights

1. **BuildTool exclusion:** The `buildable_members()` filter exists to exclude xtask-like packages from normal build operations. This is essential for keeping build commands fast and focused.

2. **Test package special handling:** Rather than running `cargo test` on Test packages, `test_all()` builds them once then discovers and runs each binary individually. This allows for parallel test execution at the binary level.

3. **Path-relative classification:** The bug fix in dev3 (`5c24d4b`) changed classification to use paths *relative to workspace root*, preventing fixtures under `tests/fixtures/` from being misclassified as Test packages.

4. **App vs Tool distinction:** Both are runnable, but `runnable_members()` only returns `App` packages. This means tool packages must be explicitly built or run, not included in workspace batch operations.

5. **POP handling:** Single-package projects always classify the single package as `App`, bypassing `classify_package()` entirely (line 72-73 in workspace.rs).
